# STYLE SAMPLE IN SECTIONS
# The narrator imitates this writing. Each section starts with a line "== tags" and shows ONE KIND of passage.
# Before each part of a beat, the program shows the narrator the section that fits what it is about to write (a
# strike from the attacker's side, the same strike from the side of the one who is hit, a pin, an escape, a
# dodge, the aftermath...), and a second one if it also fits and there is room (narration -> style_sample_chars
# characters in all). The narrator is told the passages come from a different fight and never to copy their
# events or lines; a sentence it copies word for word is cut.
# Edit freely, add sections, or give a section more tags (comma-separated). Lines starting with # are notes.
# SEVERAL sections may answer to the same tag: the program takes turns among them, so the narrator is not shown
# the same passage every time. A passage that names a fighter who is not in the current fight is skipped.
# Tags the program asks for: opening; strike act, strike take, strike take light (the part ends minor or sore), strike take mid (hurting or very painful), strike take hard (a blow that leaves a part excruciating or worse), strike take worn (the one hit is tired or spent overall), pinned take (a blow on a fighter who is pinned, held or down, with nowhere to go), knockdown take (the blow puts her down); chain; breaking point; sustain take; grab, grab take;
# pummel; throw, throw take; getup; dodge; pin start, pin start take; pin hold, pin struggle; escape; faint;
# aftermath, aftermath take; clash, clash take; dive, dive take; sputter take; coil, coil take; escape aftermath.
# super effective act, super effective take (an attack her body has no answer for); resisted act, resisted take (one
# her body is made to take, or that can't touch her).
# A tag nothing answers to falls back to the next one asked for, then to the first section.
# CONDITIONS (build 176): after the tags, "| has_b: wings" shows a passage only when the one it happens to has that
# feature, "| has_a: coils" only when the one who acts has it, "| has: fins" when anyone in the fight has it, "| kind:
# fire, ice" only when a move of that kind (or type) is in the beat. Features: wings, feathers, beak, talons, coils,
# tail, tails, fins, horn, mane, scales, shell, spikes, arms, forelegs, legs, ears, crest, flame. Kinds: bite, claw,
# punch, charge, tail, beam, horn, wing, beak, talon, throw, grab, fire, ice, wind, rock, and every move type
# (electric, water, psychic, poison, dark, steel, fighting...). A passage with no conditions is shown in any fight.
# {A} and {B} in a passage ({A} the one who acts in it, {B} the one it happens to) are shown as two names that belong
# to no fighter (Wren and Sorrel), so such a passage is shown in EVERY fight, whoever is in it. Passages that name a
# real fighter are shown only when she is in the fight.
# These passages come from a test fight played on the real engine (the sleek Ripples: no flotation sac; no pin
# clock; pain never numb), so nothing in them contradicts the program's rules.
# Your earlier samples are untouched: /sample lists them and switches back (/sample style_sample.txt).

== opening
The cave kept its own time. Water gathered at the tips of the short stalactites, swelled, and let go, and each drop struck the pools with a small bright sound that the walls handed back twice. Grey daylight came down through the crack high in the east wall and lay across the central ledge in a long pale stripe, showing every grain of the dry, gritty rock. Beyond the stripe the stone darkened toward the water and took on a green shine where the algae grew. The air tasted of salt and wet stone.

Nocturne came in along the east wall, between the fallen boulders, with the heavy rolling pace of her kind. White fur, dark blue-gray face, the scythe of her horn catching the light as her head turned. She stopped at the edge of the dry rock and looked at the ankle-deep pool in front of her the way she might have looked at something dead. Her lip lifted from one fang. She set each broad paw down on the gritty stone as if she were choosing it, and the dewclaw spikes at her heels scraped once.

*Dry ground. Keep it.*

The deep channel along the west wall bulged, and Ripples came up out of it without a splash, low and narrow, orange fur sleeked flat to her body. She climbed onto the algae-slick stone on her two feet, shook herself from ears to tail-tips, and stood there dripping, slim-necked, the cream of her throat pale in the grey light. Her twin tails began to turn behind her, lazily. The blue fins on her forearms opened and closed once.

Her black eyes went over the Absol from horn to sickle tail and stopped at the paws planted so carefully on the dry rock. The black marks on her cheeks twitched.

*Doesn't like getting her feet wet. Good to know.*

She tucked her chin a little, out of habit, and curled her webbed paws.

Nocturne's red eyes narrowed. The ruff along her neck rose, hair by hair, and her sickle tail went still.

For the space of three drops falling, neither of them moved.

== strike act, chain
Nocturne crossed the ledge at a run, keeping to the dry grit, and was over the rubble before the dust had settled.

The Buizel was on her back, watching her come. Left arm held dead still. Stomach drawn in. Both eyes on the horn.

*Looking at the wrong end of me.*

It was nearly funny. Nocturne did not use the horn. Her right forepaw came up from below the Buizel's line of sight, flat and fast, aimed at the cream of the left cheek, a cuff meant for nothing but to turn that head and take the eyes off her.

She was already moving through it. As the paw swung, her weight rolled onto her hind legs and her left forepaw rose with the claws spread, three pale hooks over the middle of the orange chest where the dark had done its work. One motion fed the next: the cuff opened the door and the Slash went through it, raking down and across, wide enough to catch the upper arms as they tried to close.

No pause. Her head followed her claws down. With the Buizel's guard dragged high, the legs lay open, and Nocturne's jaws parted over the right thigh, close enough to smell wet fur and river mud, lips peeled back from her fangs.

*Stay down a while longer.*

Her teeth closed.

== strike take
The cuff rocked her whole head sideways on the stone. Her left cheek went hot and tight, her left ear rang flat against her skull, and the heel of the paw clipped her throat on the way past so that the breath she was halfway through caught and came out as a cough.

She never saw the claws. They came down across her chest through the place the dark had already beaten tender, and the three lines they drew were not the worst of it. The worst was underneath, where the bruised muscle took the weight of the rake and something in her simply could not hold it in. The cry burst out of her, high and cracked, and went on a beat longer than she would ever have allowed. Her upper arms, snatching up too late, took the tail of the stroke, left and right, two hard stinging jars.

Then teeth, in the meat of her right thigh. A deep pinch, a shove that knocked the hip against the rock and slapped her twin tails flat. And gone again.

*Watched the horn. Idiot.*

The lateness in her limbs was lifting. Her paw closed when she told it to.

== getup
She rolled onto her good side and got the right leg under her. The thigh shook, the cave swung like a door, and she sat back down hard among the rubble.

Not here, then. She set her teeth, hauled herself across the grit on her right arm to the nearest fallen boulder, hooked both paws over its top and climbed it, left shoulder shrieking at every pull, right thigh trembling under her. She came upright against the rock and stayed there, leaning on it, ribs heaving in small careful breaths around her chest, weight off the right leg, left arm tucked to her side.

Standing. Black eyes on the Absol.

== breaking point, sustain take, fall
It hit Ripples like a door slammed on her whole front.

Her chest took it first, a flat heavy shove that emptied her lungs in one bark. Then her stomach, lower and far worse, a blow that folded her over her own middle. Her left shoulder was wrenched back by the edge of it. Her feet left the algae.

She did not get the breath back. The dark kept coming. A second ring drove into the same three places and carried her backward across the ledge until stone stopped her: the stalagmite, square between her shoulder blades, its rough flank grinding her upper back, her left hip, and that left shoulder again, caught now between the push in front and the rock behind.

*Off. Get off it.*

Her webbed paws scrabbled at nothing. Her forearm fins clamped flat.

The third ring found her stomach already clenched around the first two, and there the pain crossed over. It stopped being a blow and became a hot, sick, tearing cramp that filled her from ribs to hips, and the sound that came out of her was a thin, shocked squeal she had not chosen to make.

Then the stone behind her cracked.

The stalagmite went over with a grinding report and Ripples went with it, down through the pieces. Her left shoulder met the stump on the way and the joint lit white, a deep wrenching blaze that tore a second cry out of her, hoarser than the first. She landed flat on her back among the rubble, face to the dripping ceiling.

The dark stopped.

She lay there, ribs jerking, her bare throat working as she gulped at air that came in sips. Her left arm she did not move at all. Everything she told her body to do arrived a half-step late.

*That's one to her. Fine.*

Her black eyes found the Absol across the ledge and stayed there.

== grab
So she stopped trying to get away from the Absol and looked instead at what the Absol had put within reach. The right foreleg, planted on the grit a tail's length from her hip, braced and still while its owner got her breath back.

*You keep your feet dry. I'll keep one of them.*

Her twin tails had been hanging slack. She let them hang a moment longer, then whipped them round from behind the boulder, low, both together. They met fur above the elbow spike and went on round, once, twice, the cream tips crossing, and she hauled them tight the way she would cinch a root in a current. The white leg came toward her. The rest of the Absol came with it.

Close. A paw's length. She could see the wet red of the eyes, the grey skin of the nose, each separate fang.

She did not wait to see what those would do. She pulled a breath down past the ache in her chest, felt the cold gather at the back of her throat, and aimed at the middle of that dark face, muzzle, nose and the white throat under it.

Water Gun.

She opened her mouth and let it go.

== grab take, sustain take
The water took Nocturne full in the face from no distance at all.

Her muzzle went first, shoved sideways hard enough to drag her head round. The jet walked up it and found her nose, and the tender grey skin took the whole column of it; her eyes slammed shut and streamed. Then it dropped to her throat under the jaw and her breath stopped there, cut off in a wet bark.

She wrenched back. The leg did not come. The tails wound above her elbow held, tight and springy, and the wrench only pulled the Buizel a half-step with her, still pouring.

It did not let up. A second surge, a third, hammering the same three places, and somewhere in the second her nose stopped being a thing that hurt and became the only thing there was, a raw, blazing point she could not flinch away from because her whole face was inside it. The sound she made was not a growl. It was a high, cracked whine through clenched fangs, and she heard it and hated it. Her muzzle followed a breath later, a deep pounding heat from lip to eye that made her jaw shake. Each swallow scraped.

Then it stopped, and she was standing in her own dripping, white fur plastered flat and heavy, water running off her ruff onto the dry grit she had been so careful to keep. Tethered by one foreleg. A paw's length from the thing that had done it.

*The tails. Get the leg out first, then her.*

She forced one red eye open, a slit, and found the Buizel through the water.

== pummel
Ripples did not give her time to find the leg.

Her tails stayed cinched above the Absol's elbow, and she used them the way she would use a rope: hauled, and the dripping white head came down and in, close enough that there was no room to swing. She did not need room. Her right paw came up edge-on, webbing folded tight, and chopped into the hinge of the jaw. Brick Break.

Short. No wind-up. She drew it back the width of her own chest and chopped again into the same place, and a third time, each one driven from the hip she could still trust, her left arm tucked out of the way against her side.

*Three for one. Call it even.*

Nocturne could not see her properly and could not get away. She could feel exactly where she was, though: the pull on her foreleg told her. Her free left forepaw left the grit with the claws spread, and the dark came up along them, three curved edges of shadow longer than the claws themselves. She did not aim for the face. She aimed low, at the drawn-in middle she had already hurt, and raked across it from no distance at all.

Night Slash.

The shadow-edged claws crossed the cream of the Buizel's stomach and dragged on, up over the left hip and the lower ribs of her chest.

== throw
The tails would not come off. Very well.

Nocturne stopped pulling against them. Through the water in her eyes she could make out the Buizel bent over her own middle, slow, breath gone, and the back of the slim orange neck right there under her chin with nothing round it at all.

*If she won't let go of me, she can come with me.*

Opening her mouth cost her. The hinge of her jaw flared white the moment she spread it, and her sore muzzle and the raw point of her nose flared with it, and she did it anyway, lips skinned back, a hiss whistling out through her fangs. She closed her teeth in the loose fur and skin of the scruff, not to bite, only to hold, and the ache of clamping down ran up into her skull and stayed there.

Then she set her three free paws, dug the heel-spikes of her hind legs into the grit, and turned her whole body from the hips.

The Buizel was light. She came off her feet at once, legs trailing, and the bound foreleg came up with her, dragged by her own tails. Nocturne swung her round through a half-circle, wet white ruff flying, faced the west wall where the rock stood sheer above the channel, and at the top of the turn opened her jaws.

The weight left her mouth. The tails tore loose from her foreleg with a wet slap.

== throw take, tumble
Ripples had time for one thought in the air, and it was not a useful one.

*Oh, come on.*

The west wall met her high on the back. The flat of it took her between the shoulder blades, a blunt whole-body knock, and the left shoulder went into the rock a moment after with all her weight behind it.

That was the one. The joint had been screaming since the stalagmite; now it stopped being a joint and became a white, howling thing fastened to her side. The cry it tore out of her bounced off the low roof and came back to her. Her arm hung from it and shook, and when her fingers twitched the whole shoulder answered with a jolt that made her whimper through her teeth.

She dropped. The algae-slick stone caught her right hip and thigh, a hard wet smack on the leg that was already bitten, and it did not hold her. She went on, skidding on her side over the green rock and out onto the dry, the cave turning round her, roof, wall, roof, her right shoulder bumping, then her left thigh, then the small of her back scraping grit, each a small knock she barely counted.

She slid to a stop in the open, well out from the wall, flat on her back with her feet toward the channel.

The slowness that had held her since the claws was gone. Everything arrived on time now, all of it at once.

She lay with her ribs fluttering and her right paw closed over nothing, her twin tails loose on the stone, and turned her head until she had the Absol in sight again.

== dodge
Nocturne had been late seeing it. She had been minding her ribs, and the Buizel was already three strides into the rush before her head came round.

*Looking at yourself. Stupid.*

There was no time to brace and no sense in it. She took her weight off her forelegs and let her whole front swing left, away from the bad side, hindquarters following a moment behind. The move pulled across her right ribs like a hook dragged through them, and she let her breath go rather than hold it against that.

The water went by her chest close enough to wet it. Spray spattered her open eye. She felt the wind of the small body along the whole of her right flank, saw the whirling tails pass a whisker from her ruff, and then there was nothing in front of her but the stripe of daylight on the rock.

The Buizel shot on past, six, seven strides, the jet tearing itself to mist around her as she fought it to a stop. She came down on her heels at the edge of the shallows, turned on the good leg, and stood there with water running off her fins, sides going hard. The right paw she had cocked for the next blow was still raised. She looked at it, and lowered it.

Nocturne set her feet again, right elbow tight to her side. Her jaw ached where it sagged. She breathed in short pulls and kept her horn between them.

Neither moved. Somewhere behind, a drop fell into the channel.

== pin start
Nocturne let go of the foot and did not straighten up.

*Both legs hurt now. Take them away and she has nowhere to stand. Then the same four places as before, and this time mind the left foot.*

The Buizel was backing into the shallows, arms out, both feet in a finger's depth of water over slick stone. Nocturne came in under those arms with her right side turned away, to spare her ribs, and put her left shoulder into the orange belly, low, pushing, not striking. At the same moment she hooked a forepaw behind the left knee, the leg she had just taken the strength out of, and drew it toward her.

It went. The small body tipped backward off its feet and Nocturne followed it down, her forelegs straddling it, so that the Buizel landed on her back in the shallow water with a slap and the Absol was already over her.

Cold water closed round Nocturne's paws. She hated it, and stayed.

She placed herself in the order she had planned. Left hind paw on the left thigh, where the muscle was still jumping. Right hind paw on the right thigh, over the old bite. Then her chest, the full weight of it and the soaked ruff, laid across the cream ribs until the water squeezed out from between them. Last, her left forepaw, set flat across the bare throat below the jaw, pad down, claws clear.

She pressed, and waited to feel what pressed back.

== pin start take
The water was the only kind thing about it.

It lay a finger deep under Ripples' back, cold along her spine and round her ears, and for a breath she simply let it be there. The fog that the horn had knocked into her thinned and went. She knew where all of her was again.

All of her was under an Absol.

*Twice in one fight. I should charge her rent.*

The paws on her thighs were not alike. The right one sat on the bite and made it throb in slow, dull pushes. The left sat on the place the horn had just crossed, and that was worse: a hot, sick ache that spread under the pad whenever the big body shifted. She tried to turn her knees out and neither one answered.

The chest came down on her own chest like a wet sack of stones. Her ribs had nothing left to say about it that they had not said already; they only creaked, and her breath went shallow and quick, high in her neck.

The forepaw lay across her throat. It was not heavy. It was just there, flat and warm and exactly wide enough, and each breath had to squeeze past under it.

Both her arms were free this time, the good one and the bad one. Her tails were trapped under her in the water but could still stir. She let them stir, slowly, feeling the cold move through them.

Above her the dark face dripped onto her cheek. The mouth was open, crooked at the hinge. One red eye watched her left foot.

== pin hold
Nocturne had not expected to be this tired.

Standing in a hand's depth of cold water with all four paws busy was not resting. Her right ribs caught on every breath, a hard stitch under the foreleg, and her open mouth had gone dry at the front and wet at the back. She locked her elbows, because if she did not they would begin to shake, and she was not going to shake on top of something half her size.

*Stand on her as if it were nothing. It is nothing.*

Her hind paws stayed where she had set them. Under the left one the cut thigh had stopped jumping; under the right, the bitten one lay heavy and still. She kept her pads spread on both and her claws out of the fur.

Her weight she had stopped thinking about. It was simply down there, on the cream chest, and the chest under it rose a little and fell a little, and each rise was smaller than the one before.

It was the forepaw she watched. She had laid it across the throat lightly, and it had not stayed light; leaning forward to keep her balance in the water had put more of her onto it than she meant. Under the pad she could feel the breath go by, thin as a thread drawn through a hole, and the small hard knot of the windpipe move when the Buizel tried to swallow and could not quite. The cream fur there was dark with water. A pulse beat against her wrist, quick, quicker than her own.

She did not ease it. She did not lean harder either.

The face below her had changed. The black eyes that had been measuring her were not measuring now. They slid half shut, dragged open, found her horn, lost it. The arms that had been free and busy lay out in the water with the fins flat and the paws open. Only the tails still moved, a slow stir that sent small rings out past Nocturne's forelegs.

Then the rings stopped, and she felt the hips under her tighten.

== pin struggle
Ripples had been somewhere quiet, and came back because her throat hurt.

The world had gone small: the paw under her chin, the thin cold whistle of air getting past it, a grey edge round everything else. She knew she had been meaning to do something. It took her a moment to find it.

*Turn. Get a shoulder under. Left side, away from the bad arm. No. The bad arm is the left. Right, then.*

She gathered what there was. It was not much, and it came slowly, like water filling a footprint. She pressed her right elbow into the stone under the shallows, set her teeth, and rolled her whole body toward it, hips first, hard.

For a moment the weight on her chest slid. The hind paw on her right thigh skated in the wet fur.

The other one did not. Nocturne had felt the hips tighten, and her left hind paw came down into the left thigh with the Absol's haunch behind it, straight onto the stripe the horn had left.

It was a slow thing, that pain, not a sharp one. First only pressure, the pad spreading. Then the bruised muscle underneath gave up its place and the ache came up from deep in the leg, thick and hot, filling the thigh from the knee to the hip like something poured. Her breath stopped in her nose. A sound got out anyway, a small cracked gasp with no voice behind it, and her foot kicked once in the water and lay still. The roll went out of her. Her hips sank back flat. She could not have said whether she had decided to stop.

*That was all of it. That was everything, and she just stood on it.*

She looked up for the red eye and found it, and could not hold it. Her own lids kept coming down. She pushed them open; they came down again, heavier. The cave roof swam, the daylight stripe on it doubling and sliding back together.

Her arms lay out in the shallows where she had left them. Her tails had stopped stirring. Under the forepaw her breath went in and out in threads, and the water rocked gently against her ears.

== escape
Ripples had filled herself to the bottom, ribs screaming, and now she spent all of it at once.

Her tails did the first part. Flattened under her back, they had been winding tight against the stone; she let them go, and they snapped straight and threw her hips a paw's width off the ground. The quiet paw on her left thigh slid. Her left leg came out from under it, knee to her chest, and she drove the flat of her webbed foot into the Absol's right side with everything that breath had bought her.

It landed on the ribs, low, just behind the foreleg, with a thick, hollow thump.

Nocturne's breath left her in one bark. The whole right side of her chest clenched round the place like a fist, a deep, hot, stabbing ache that caught again the moment she tried to breathe in. She came off the small body sideways, three stiff steps, claws scraping, and stood with her head low and her right elbow drawn in against her side, taking air in short, careful halves.

*Out. Up, before she turns round.*

== faint
The drip was the last thing Ripples kept hold of.

She had lost the cave roof some time ago, and then the red eye, and then the cold of the water, which was a pity, because she had liked it. What stayed was sound. Her own breath, very far down, a thread going in and a thread going out. The Absol's, big and rough, above. And the drip, falling into the channel she had never got back to.

*Nearly had her. Twice. Tell them that.*

There was nobody to tell. The thought came apart before it was finished.

She tried once more to lift her eyelids and they did not answer, and it did not seem to matter. The ache in her thigh and the weight on her chest went a long way off, as if they belonged to somebody on the far bank. Her paws opened in the water. Her tails, which had not stopped moving all fight, lay down side by side.

Her head rolled a little toward her good shoulder, and stayed there. Her eyes were closed. Her ribs went on rising and falling, small and even, under the white chest.

Nocturne felt it go out of her like a held breath let loose: the last tightness in the hips, the faint push of the throat against her pad, gone, both at once.

She did not move.

She had been kicked off this animal once already for believing what she felt. So she kept her forepaw exactly where it was and her weight exactly where it was, and watched the shut eyes, and listened. One breath. Another. She pressed nothing harder. She only waited for a fin to twitch, a tail to curl, a knee to come up under her.

Nothing came.

She lifted the forepaw first, and the cream throat under it filled and fell, easier. Then her chest, slowly, her ribs paying for every inch. Then one hind paw and the other, stepping back off the thighs into the shallows, until she stood on her own four feet with nothing under her.

Water ran off her ruff. The Buizel lay on her back in a finger's depth of it, breathing.

Nocturne looked at her for a long moment, mouth open, sides heaving, and then looked away at the stripe of daylight on the wall.

== aftermath
Nocturne walked out of the water before she did anything else.

It was only six steps to dry grit and she took them one at a time, shaking each paw as it came clear, and stood on the ledge with the wet weight of her ruff pulling at her neck. The cave smelled of salt and cold stone and, faintly, of herself. She did not shake herself dry. That would have gone through her ribs.

The fight let go of her in pieces. Her breath slowed first, though every one still snagged at the same place under the right foreleg, a hard, sore catch she was already learning to breathe round. The foreleg above it, where the tails had been, only ached like an old bruise. Her throat was raw to the bottom; swallowing was a thing to plan.

Her face was the worst of it. With nothing left to do, it had her whole attention: the nose a hot, fat, stopped-up throb, the muzzle tight and tender to the roots of her whiskers, and the jaw simply wrong, sagging at the hinge, heavy, aching in a slow pulse. She tried, carefully, to close her mouth. It closed part of the way and told her not to try again.

*Beaten about the face by a jet of water and one small paw. If anyone asks, it was a much larger Buizel.*

She thought of the moment by the boulder, the twin tails whipping round her leg out of nowhere, and her ears went back.

Then she turned to look.

== aftermath take
The Buizel lay where she had been left, on her back in the shallows at the ledge's edge, and the water had already gone still round her.

It was a finger deep and clear to the stone. It held her up a little; her orange fur lifted and settled in it along her sides, and her twin tails floated out behind her hips, side by side, their cream tips turning slowly in a current too small to see. Her arms lay open. The fins on them were folded flat. Her head was tipped toward her right shoulder with one ear under the surface.

She was breathing. Nocturne watched for that first and found it: the cream chest rising, slow and even, falling, a pause, rising again. Nothing like the fast sips from before. It was the breathing of something asleep at the bottom of a long day.

The marks of the fight were all there to read. The three lines across the chest. The left thigh swollen above the knee, the right dark with the print of teeth. The shoulder she had carried tucked in all fight lay easy now that nobody was asking anything of it.

Her eyes stayed shut. A drop fell from the roof into the channel, and another, and the rings ran out across the shallows and broke softly against the small still face.

The stripe of daylight had moved a paw's width along the wall since this began. It lay across the dry ledge between them now, pale, full of slow dust.

Nocturne stood at the edge of it, stiffly, favouring her ribs, and kept watch.

== strike take hard, breaking point
The claws found the one place she had been guarding all fight.

Ripples saw them coming and could do nothing with what she saw. Three dark edges crossed her stomach from hip to rib, over muscle that had been beaten twice already and had nothing left to give, and the world went white from the middle outward.

Her mouth opened. Nothing came out. Her eyes were squeezed so tight that tears ran from the corners into her ears, her back arched off the stone, every limb rigid, both paws open and shaking in the air, and still there was no sound, only her ribs jerking as they tried to find a breath to scream with.

Then it came, all at once, a thin cracked shriek that broke in the middle and came back as a ragged gasp.

She curled. She could not have stopped herself. Knees to chest, arms locked round her middle, forehead against her own thighs, twin tails wound tight about her ankles like something trying to hold her together. The pain did not come in waves any more. It simply stood in her, hot and sick and enormous, pushing outward against her ribs and down into her hips, and every breath she managed leaned on it and came back out as a whimper.

*Not there. Not there again.*

She heard herself think it and hated it, and the hate was the first thing that was hers.

She made her eyes open. The cave was a smear. In the middle of the smear stood a white shape with a horn, waiting, and Ripples, still folded round her stomach, still shaking, bared her small teeth at it.

== strike take hard
The paw came up under her guard, edge-on, and landed on the hinge of her jaw where two had landed before.

Nocturne heard it more than felt it, a flat knock inside her own head, and then she felt it. The whole side of her face lit from ear to chin. Her head went with the blow and her body followed her head, forelegs crossing, claws shrieking on the stone, and she stood skewed with her muzzle almost on the ground and her mouth open and could not close it.

The sound that came out of her was not a growl. It was a long, breathy keen, pitched high, shaking in time with the shaking of her jaw, and it went on until her breath ran out.

She tried to swallow. The hinge ground, hot and loose, and her eyes flooded. Spit ran in a string from her lip. Each pull of air across her tongue was its own fresh hurt, so that breathing became a thing to be got through, one breath, then the next, each one ending in a small wet catch.

Her ears were flat to her skull. Her ruff stood out in spikes and every hair trembled. Her sickle tail had curled under and wrapped her own hind leg, and she had not told it to.

*Up. Head up. She is still there.*

She lifted her head. It weighed as much as the rest of her. One red eye opened, swimming, and found the small orange shape a stride away with its paw still raised; and Nocturne, with her jaw hanging and her whole face a single pounding ache, pulled her lips back from her fangs on the side that would still do it.

== strike take
It caught her on the flank, low, behind the ribs.

Nocturne grunted, a short hard sound through shut teeth, and went sideways half a step before her claws bit and held. Water burst over her back and ran off her ruff. For a breath her right side was one flat sting, the kind that follows a slap; then the sting sank inward and became an ache she could feel the exact size of, a paw's width across, warm and already tightening.

She had been hit harder. She told herself so, and it was true.

Her ear on that side had gone flat. She put it back up. She rolled the hip once, testing: it answered, a little stiff, with a pull that she would feel again tomorrow and could ignore today. Nothing in it changed how she stood.

*Fast. Faster than she looks. Stop watching the tails and watch the shoulders.*

The Buizel had landed beyond her and spun, dripping, fins half open, pleased with herself. Nocturne turned to keep the horn between them. She did it without hurry, and she breathed out through her nose, slowly, so that the breath would not show.

Then she shook the water off, once, from ruff to tail, and the look she gave across the wet stone was the look of someone who has been handed a bill and means to pay it in full.

== strike act
Ripples did not give herself time to feel how badly she was standing.

*She watched me fall over. Fine. She can watch this too.*

Water came when she called it, cold out of the air and off the wet stone, and wrapped her from muzzle to tail-tips in a skin that hissed. Her fins went flat to her forearms. She let go of the ledge.

The first stride was ugly; her right leg took her weight and told her about it. The second was better. By the third she was not running so much as being thrown, her twin tails whirling behind her in a blur, the jet lifting her heels off the grit, the cave walls smearing past.

She had it planned out, all three parts. The rush would take the Absol in the chest, on the side she had just kicked, and fold her. Then the edge of the right paw, up under that loose jaw. Then cold, in the same place, to finish.

The white shape grew in front of her. She saw the wet spikes of the ruff, the red eyes, one of them still half shut, the head beginning to come round. She saw the chest, broad and pale behind the dark forelegs, and she put her good shoulder down and aimed herself at the middle of it.

Two strides. One.

She was close enough to smell wet fur.

== strike act, sustain act
Nocturne did not want her any closer to that channel.

She read the Buizel in one long look: the weight forward on the balls of her feet, the twin tails turning lazily but ready, the slick green stone behind her and the deep water one stride beyond it. Everything about the small orange body leaned toward the wet. Let her get back in there and the fight would happen on her ground.

*So she doesn't get to.*

She did not charge. She planted all four paws on the dry grit, spread her claws until they bit, and lowered her head so the scythe of her horn pointed at the middle of the Buizel's cream chest. The ruff along her neck stood out stiff. Something cold gathered behind her breastbone, the way a growl gathers, and climbed.

Dark Pulse.

It left her as a ring of black, soundless at first, a ripple of pure ill will that bent the grey light as it crossed the ledge. The stripe of daylight on the rock dimmed where it passed. Drops falling from the stalactites swerved in the air.

Nocturne's ears went flat with the effort. This was not a move to throw and forget; she leaned into it, shoulders bunched, and kept it coming, ring after ring, each one pushed out on a hard breath through her teeth. Her forepaws slid a finger's width back on the grit and she dug them in again.

She had aimed for the chest. The spread of it would take the stomach below and the near shoulder too, and behind the Buizel, exactly in line, a thick stalagmite stood up from the edge of the ledge like a post.

*Should have closed the distance first.* The thought came sour. From here she could not see the Buizel's eyes, only the shape of her, and she disliked not seeing what a blow did.

The first ring reached the orange fur.

== pin hold
Holding still was harder than throwing her had been.

Nocturne kept her hind paws spread on the two thighs and felt the difference between them through her pads. The left lay quiet. The right, the one her teeth had found, jumped and quivered under her, and each time it did she put a little more of her haunch behind that paw until it stopped.

Her weight she left where it was, on the chest, the whole wet drag of her ruff and the heavy bone behind it. She could feel the Buizel's breathing through her own breastbone, quick and high and small, like something trapped in a wall. She did not shift to make it easier. She did not shift at all.

The forepaw across the throat needed the most care. Too light and that chin would work under it. Too hard and she would have to look at what she was doing. She kept the bar of her wrist level and still, and under it the bare cream fur moved once as the Buizel swallowed.

It cost her. Bent over like this her head hung low, and blood beat in her face. Her nose was a hot, wet, swollen point that she could not breathe through, so she breathed through her mouth, and her mouth meant her jaw, which ached at the hinge with every pull of air and would not close. A thread of water ran off her muzzle and dripped onto orange fur. Her own throat was raw from the inside.

*Hold. That is all there is to do. Hold.*

Her sickle tail stood out stiff behind her for balance. Her ears lay flat.

Close below her were the two black eyes, bright, not pleading, flicking from her face to her shoulders and back. Counting something. The free right paw was pressed against her ruff, not pushing yet. The fins on the orange forearms had lifted a little way off the fur.

Nocturne saw that, and narrowed her one open eye.

== pin hold
The cave had gone quiet enough to hear both of them breathe.

Nocturne listened to it. Her own breath came through her open mouth in slow, rough pulls, each one whistling a little past the jaw that would not shut, and under that, quicker and far smaller, the Buizel's: short sips taken through the nose, several to every one of hers. Somewhere behind them water dripped from the roof into the channel, steady and unhurried.

She had put everything back where it belonged. Her left hind paw lay on the left thigh and barely had to work. Her right hind paw bore down on the right thigh, the bitten one, and the muscle there twitched against her pads. Her chest and the damp weight of her ruff rested on the cream ribs. Her forepaw lay across the throat, wrist level, and through it she felt each small breath pass.

Her nose still blazed from that paw. She would not sneeze. She would not shake her head, not with those black eyes a whisker's length below.

*She gets nothing from me. Not a sound.*

It was harder work than before. The orange body under her was not lying slack; it hummed, the way a branch hums before it springs back. The hips shifted a hair. The twin tails, trapped under the small of the Buizel's back, flexed against the stone. The breathing under her forepaw changed, the sips drawing out longer and deeper, as if something were being filled.

Nocturne dug her claws into the grit and leaned.

== pin struggle
Ripples had been listening to the two of them breathe. Hers, which were small, and the Absol's, which came in ragged pulls through that hanging mouth, each one ending in a tiny hitch as the jaw caught.

On the hitch, the weight above her shifted a hair toward the head.

*Now. Sideways, not up.*

She did not buck. She flattened instead, let every muscle go loose against the grit, and in the same instant shoved off the stone with her one good shoulder blade and both heels, straight toward her own right. The grit rolled under her back like shot. Her hips came half out from under the white belly. Her left thigh slid free of its paw. For one breath her chest had nothing on it and she dragged in air all the way to the bottom, and it hurt so much to fill those ribs that her sight went grey at the edges.

Her right paw was already moving. She had nothing to brace it with and no room to swing, so she simply drove the heel of it up, hard, into the middle of the face above her.

It landed square on the nose.

Nocturne made a sound Ripples had not heard from her before, a short, shocked huff, almost a sneeze, with no voice in it at all. Her head snapped up and away. Both red eyes screwed shut and ran. For a long moment she did nothing else. The swollen point of her nose had been the centre of her face since the water; now it rang, a deep, sick, spreading clang that went back through her muzzle and into her teeth, and the breath she pulled to answer it scraped down her raw throat and came out again as a whine she strangled before it was out. Her forelegs trembled. She tasted salt.

She did not let go.

She opened one eye, found the orange body half out from under her, and came down on it. The hind paw stamped back onto the left thigh and pinned it. Her chest dropped across the cream ribs again, heavier than before, and the bar of her forepaw slid back under the chin and stayed.

Ripples lay flat beneath her once more, breath squeezed back to sips, the right thigh throbbing under its paw, black eyes still bright.

== pin start
Nocturne shook herself once, hard, from horn to sickle tail. Water flew off her in a ring and pattered on the grit, and her ruff stood out again in damp spikes, lighter. The shake jarred her jaw and she swallowed the sound it wanted.

The Buizel lay in the open. Dry rock on every side of her, and the channel a long way off.

*There. Not in the water. Not near it.*

She crossed the ledge at a stiff, fast walk, head low, breathing through her nose in short pulls because her mouth would not bear much. She did not circle. She came straight up the line of the small body from the feet, saw the right thigh she had bitten and the left beside it, and stepped over both.

Her hind paws came down first, one on each thigh, broad pads spread, the heel-spikes braced against the stone to either side so the legs under them had nowhere to go.

Then she let her weight follow. She lowered her chest and the wet mass of her ruff onto the cream of the Buizel's chest, slowly, all of it, until she felt ribs take the load.

Last, her left forepaw. She set it across the bare throat just under the tucked chin, not the claws, the flat of the wrist and pad, a bar laid from one side of the slim neck to the other.

She leaned on it.

== pin start take
The stone under Ripples' back was cold and gritty, and the Absol on top of her was warm, wet and very heavy.

Her thighs first. Two broad pads, one on each, pressing the muscle flat against the rock, and the bitten right one answered with a deep, sore throb. She tried her knees. They lifted the width of a whisker and stopped.

Then the weight came onto her chest, and her chest was the worst place to put it. Every rib that the dark and the claws had worked on took its share. The pain was a wide, hot band that tightened as the load settled, and her breath, which had only just come back, was squeezed down to the top of her lungs. She breathed in small sips, quickly, through her nose.

*Heavy for something that hates getting wet.*

The forepaw across her throat was almost gentle by comparison, and she liked it least of all. She had tucked her chin a moment too late. The bar of wrist and pad lay under it, on the bare cream fur, and when she swallowed she felt her throat move against it.

Her right arm was free. She brought it up and set the heel of her paw against the white ruff, testing, and the ruff did not shift. Her left arm she left on the stone.

Above her the dark face hung close, dripping, the jaw held a little open and trembling at the hinge. One red eye, narrowed to a slit, looked down into both of hers.

She looked back.

== getup
Nothing lay on Ripples now. She threw her right arm over the lip of the ledge and hauled. Her left shoulder shrieked, her bitten thigh folded, and she dropped back onto the stone hard enough to click her teeth.

She rolled onto her front instead, got her knees under her, and pushed up along the ledge's edge with her good arm. This time she kept rising. She stood, swaying, sides heaving, her weight off the right leg and her left arm tucked against her ribs, one paw on the rock.

Four strides of wet stone lay between them.

== strike act
Seraphina did not hurry. She had never needed to.

She lay in three loose coils at the water's edge with her head raised above them, antennae still, and watched the Absol pace. The white one favoured her right foreleg. It was slight: a shorter stride on that side, the paw set down a little flat. Seraphina marked it and looked away as if she had seen nothing.

*She guards it. So she will not expect me to reach past it.*

Her tail fan lay half open in the shallows behind her, and she drew it slowly through the water, gathering weight. The pool climbed her scales. A low note began in her throat, the hum she made without meaning to when she was pleased.

The Absol turned at the end of her line, and for that one stride her flank was toward the water.

Seraphina's coils closed like a hand. All the length of her went taut at once, head dropping for balance, and the tail came out of the pool in a long rising arc, fan spread wide and edged with water that hung in the air behind it like glass. She put the whole of her spine into the turn. Scale slid on scale with a dry hiss.

It was not aimed at the foreleg. It was aimed past it, at the ribs behind, where the white fur lay flat and thought itself safe.

The fan came round.

== grab take
The coil went round her waist before Ripples had finished landing.

It was not fast. That was the worst of it. It slid, cool and dry and heavy, one loop, and a second above it, and each scale caught on her fur the wrong way as it tightened. Then the slack went out all at once and her feet left the ground.

Her ribs were squeezed inward. Not far; far enough. The breath she had was pushed up into her throat and out in a squeak, and the next one would not go in past the top of her chest. Her arms were free. She beat at the coils with both paws and it was like beating a wet tree.

*Slippery. She's slippery. Find the gap. There's always a gap.*

Above her the long pale head swung down to look, unhurried, antennae drifting. The red eyes were mild. A low humming came through the coils into Ripples' own body, steady as a held note, and with every breath Ripples let out, the loops took up the room it left.

Her tails were outside. Both of them. She felt them trailing, forgotten, and something small and hard and delighted woke up behind the fear.

She let her arms go slack. She let her head fall back, eyes half shut, and breathed in the little sips that were all she could get. And behind the Milotic's line of sight, slowly, so that no ripple showed, her twin tails began to turn.

== clash
The Water Gun came off the shallows in a hard white rope, straight at the Absol's face, and Nocturne did not move out of its way.

She opened her jaws instead. The Dark Pulse went out of her in a ring that bent the light, and the two met in the air halfway across the ledge with a sound like a door the size of the cave being slammed. Spray went out sideways in a sheet. Grit lifted off the stone in a circle and hung there.

For a long moment nobody was hit by anything. Nocturne leaned into it with her forepaws braced and her ears flat, feeling the push of the water come back up through her own attack like a weight against a door. Her horn caught the grey light. Her lip peeled off her fangs.

*Harder than she looks. Not hard enough.*

The ring went through. What was left of the jet broke apart round it and fell as rain on the rock, and the dark rolled on, thinner now, toward the Buizel's chest.

== clash take
The jet left her and hit something.

Ripples felt it before she saw it: the pressure coming back up her own stream into her open mouth and her braced arms, the water stalling against a wall that was not there. Then the dark ring showed through the spray, flattening the stream in front of it, and she had no breath left to push with.

It broke. The water fell apart in front of her face and the rest of the dark came through and took her across the chest and the left shoulder, weaker than it had been, but enough. Her fins snapped flat. The breath went out of her in a grunt.

*Met it head-on. She met it head-on.*

Her chest stung where it had landed. Her jaw ached from holding the stream. She shook the water out of her eyes and kept them on the Absol, who was still standing in the same place, ears flat, as if nothing had happened at all.

== dive
Talon climbed until the ledge was a grey shelf far below her and the Absol on it was a white mark with a dark horn, looking up.

She hung there a moment on half-spread wings, the wind combing her crest flat, choosing. Then she folded.

The air rose round her to a shriek. The ground came up, the white mark grew legs and a face, and she saw the head turn, too late, to find her in the light. At the last instant she flared, wings cracking open, talons swinging forward, and the whole weight of the drop went into the blow.

Her talons struck the Absol's back and drove her down. The impact shocked up through her own legs into her hips. She was already beating again before her feet had left the fur, three hard downstrokes, grit blasting out from under her, and she was up and out of reach with the Absol's snap closing on nothing.

*Again. And again. Until she stops looking up.*

== dive take
There was a shadow on the stone, and then there was no time.

The weight came down on Nocturne's back like a falling boulder with claws in it. Her legs folded. Her chest hit the grit and the air went out of her in one flat grunt, and something hooked deep into the meat over her spine and tore free.

By the time she got her head round, snapping, the bird was already rising, the downdraft of the wings flattening the fur on her neck. Her jaws closed on air and a single grey feather.

*Above me. Always above me.*

Her back burned in two long lines where the talons had gone in. She got her legs under her and stood, and the burn spread down into her hips with every breath. She did not take her eyes off the sky again.

== sputter take
The water came straight into her open mouth.

It went down the wrong way at once, up the back of her nose and into her throat, and every part of Ripples forgot the fight. She coughed so hard her whole body bent with it. Water came out of her nose. She tried to breathe in and the breath caught on what was still in there and turned into another cough, and another, harsh and wet, her eyes streaming.

*Breathe. Breathe, idiot.*

She spat, gagged, spat again. Her chest heaved against it. When the air finally came it came in ragged and thin, and she could hear herself wheezing, and she knew exactly how open she was, doubled over on the wet stone with her eyes full of water and her guard nowhere.

It passed. Not all at once. Her throat stayed raw, and every breath for a while after scraped on the way in.

== coil
Seraphina did not hurry. She let the loop slide round under the Buizel's arms, unhurried, a cool weight of scales, and only when it was all the way round did she close it.

She felt the body inside the coil go rigid, then push. She took the push and gave nothing back. On the next breath out she tightened again, a little, just enough to take the room that breath had left behind.

Her head rose above it all, swaying slightly, red eyes half-closed. The hair-like fins along her head lay smooth.

*There is no hurry. There is never any hurry.*

Inside the coils, against her own scales, she could feel the other heart. Quick at first, hammering. Then a little slower. Then slower still.

== coil take
The coil closed and kept closing.

It was not like being hit. Nothing struck her. There was only the weight, warm and patient, round her chest and her arms, and each time she breathed out it took the room the breath had used and did not give it back.

Her paws were going cold first. Then heavy. She could feel her own heartbeat in them, a slow, thick thud, slower than it should have been, as if her blood had to push its way through.

*Push. Push against it.*

She pushed. The scales took it and gave nothing. The grey was coming in at the edges of her sight now, soft, and the beat in her ears was dragging, a long gap between each one. Still going. Slow. Slow.

== escape aftermath
She was out, and her body did not seem to know it yet.

Ripples got her feet under her on the second try. Her back was stiff from the long press of the stone, and when she turned her head the muscles down the side of her neck pulled tight and held. Her right arm prickled, then burned, as the blood came back into it in a rush of pins and needles. She shook it out, hissing.

*Stiff. Slow. She'll see it.*

She did not go back in. She backed off a step, then another, and watched the Absol rise off the stone. She knew the shape of that hold now: the paw across the throat, the weight on the chest, the way it had shifted just before it tightened. She would see it coming next time.

Her legs were steadier by the time she stopped. Not steady. Steadier.

== dwell
It did not stop when the claws left her.

That was the thing Ripples had not expected. The rake across her stomach was over, the Absol's paw already gone, and still the hurt kept arriving: a first hot line, then a second wave underneath it, deeper, where the earlier blows had left the muscle soft, then a third that seemed to come up from inside her and spread out sideways into her hips.

She found she had curled round it. She did not remember deciding to. Her arms were drawn in tight across her belly and her knees had come up, and the cream fur under her paws was hot and wrong to the touch.

*Breathe. Small ones.*

The small ones were bearable. A big one pushed her stomach out against the wound and lit the whole thing up again, and a sound got out of her before she could stop it, a thin whine through her shut teeth. She clamped her jaw on the rest. Her cheek marks twitched. A tremor started low in her belly and ran out through her arms, fine and fast, and she pressed her paws harder against herself to stop it and could not.

Cold sweat under the fur. Her tails lay flat on the stone behind her and would not lift.

She made herself open her eyes. The Absol was still there, a white shape against the grey light, watching her.

== watch
Nocturne did not follow it up. She stood over the Buizel with her forepaws set wide and her own breath coming hard, and she looked.

The orange body had folded round its stomach. That was the first thing. Not the arms, not the head: the stomach, exactly where the claws had gone. *So that's the one.* The arms were tight across it, the knees drawn up, and as she watched, a shiver started somewhere under the cream fur and ran out to the tips of the webbed paws and came back.

She tilted her head. The Buizel's breath was quick and shallow and high in the chest, every one cut short before it reached the belly. Every third or fourth one caught, a small hitch, and with it the jaw clenched and a sound tried to get out and was swallowed. Nocturne heard it anyway. A thin, high thread of a noise, there and gone.

*Trying not to let me hear. Too late.*

The twin tails had stopped moving. That told her more than the rest. The black eyes opened, found her, and held, and there was still fight in them, but slower now, the way a fire is slower when it has burned down.

Her own shoulder throbbed where the ice had bitten it. She shifted her weight off it and kept watching. She wanted to see all of it.

== devastating
Nocturne saw the opening before she understood it. The Buizel had committed to the dodge left, all her weight on one webbed foot, and the stomach that had already taken three rakes was turned square toward her, guard nowhere.

She did not think. Her whole body went into it, the push off her hind legs, the twist through her spine, the forepaw coming round low and flat, and she felt the exact moment the claws arrived at the place they had been before, the soft, swollen centre of it.

It did not feel like the other blows. The others had met muscle, fur, resistance. This one met nothing. It went in, and in, and she felt the whole orange body fold round it like wet paper.

*That one. That went all the way.*

She was almost startled. Then a hard, cold satisfaction came up through her, and she held the paw there half a heartbeat longer than she needed to, just to be sure of it.

== devastating take
She knew it was wrong before it finished landing.

Ripples had taken the rakes on her stomach before. She knew how they hurt. This did not hurt like that. It went past the bruises and the torn-up fur and found something underneath them all, the deepest, rawest place, and arrived there with everything behind it.

The sound that came out of her was not one she knew she could make. It tore up out of her chest and rang off the cave walls and came back, and she couldn't stop it, couldn't close her mouth on it, and when it ran out there was no breath behind it at all.

Her legs went. She did not decide to fold; her body simply stopped holding her up, and she was curled round it on the wet stone, every muscle locked, a deep hard shudder running out from her middle to the tips of her tails.

*Something's wrong. Something's really wrong.*

The colour had gone out of the cave. There was the pain, and a high thin ringing, and the white shape of the Absol above her, and nothing else at all.

# ---- build 123: more passages to rotate through (light, mid and knockdown takes; attacks, dodges, throws, pins, get-ups) ----

== strike act
The sand was the trouble, and Nocturne had known it from the moment the clearing opened onto the beach.

It was pale and soft and dry on top, and it gave under a broad paw like flour. She did not go onto it. She kept to the edge of the grass, where the roots of the old oak broke the surface and the ground held, and paced along it with her head low and her horn turned toward the water.

The Buizel stood ankle-deep in the warm shallows, a stride out from the sand, her twin tails turning slowly behind her for balance. The fins on her forearms were up. Her small black eyes went from Nocturne's face to her paws and back, and once, quickly, over her own shoulder to the deeper water behind her.

*Thinking about leaving.*

That glance was the opening. For the length of it, the Buizel's weight had shifted onto her back foot and her near arm had drifted down from her chest.

Nocturne went. A bound across the sand, low, the dry grains spraying out behind her, then another, and with that one she was already rising onto her hind legs, the heel spikes digging into the wet margin where beach met lake. Her right forepaw came up and back past her ear. The claws spread, pale and curved, and the paw came down and across in one long rake aimed at the orange shoulder and the cream of the chest below it.

The Buizel's head came round. Too slow. Nocturne saw the black eyes widen and the arm start up to cover, and then she was there, close enough to feel the warmth off the wet fur, and her claws met it.

== strike act
Rain came across the clearing in slanting grey sheets, and Nocturne stood in it as if it were not there.

It was there. It ran off her horn and dripped from her chin and soaked her ruff into a cold, heavy collar, and every part of her that was not fighting wanted to be under the pines at the clearing's edge. She put that part away.

The Buizel loved it. That much was plain. She stood in the middle of the flattened grass with her face tipped up, her orange fur dark and sleek, rain beading on the cream spots above her eyes, her twin tails flicking water as they turned. She was waiting for the thunder. Each time it came, she flinched a little toward it, and her guard opened a hair.

*Then let the sky choose the moment.*

Nocturne lowered her head and waited. She felt the dark come up into her forepaws the way cold comes up through stone, slow at first, then all at once, and when she looked down, the claws of her right paw were edged in it, longer than they were, a faint smoke of shadow that the rain fell straight through.

Lightning lit the clearing. The Buizel's chin lifted.

Nocturne was moving before the thunder arrived. She crossed the wet grass in a low, rolling rush, keeping her paws flat so they would not slide, and the boom broke over them both as she came in under the raised arms. Her right forepaw swept up from below, shadow-claws wide, aimed at the left side of the cream belly where the ribs ended.

The Buizel twisted. Too late to go anywhere. The dark edges touched wet fur.

== strike act
Mud was not water, Nocturne told herself, and that was the only reason she had come this far into it.

It took her to the wrists at every step, warm and brown and sucking, and let go of each paw with a wet clap. Reeds stood up in clumps. A drowned log lay half sunk on her left, its bark gone soft and black. The air hummed with insects and smelled of rot.

The Buizel had chosen this place. She was quicker in it than Nocturne, light enough to skim where the white body sank, and she had been darting in and out since the fight began, clawing, then backing off into the reeds.

Now she was backing again, and the mud had her.

Nocturne saw it happen. The left foot went down into a soft place and did not come up when it was asked. The Buizel's arms flew out. Her twin tails whirled behind her, trying to hold her upright, and for that moment all of her attention was on her own feet.

*Stuck. Stay stuck.*

Nocturne did not run; nothing could run here. She lunged. Every paw drove down at once and she threw herself forward along the top of the mud, body flat, forelegs reaching, the drowned log sliding by at her shoulder. Her jaws opened wide. The orange arm nearest her, the right one, with its blue fin flared and dripping, swung up toward her face as the Buizel caught her balance and saw her coming.

That was what Nocturne wanted. She turned her head a little to the side so the horn would clear, and her teeth closed over the meat of the upper arm, above the fin.

== strike act
The ice boomed somewhere far out under the snow, a long low note that Nocturne felt in the pads of every paw.

She did not like it. She liked the Buizel less. The little animal had found her feet out here faster than she had any right to, skating where she should have walked, and more than once she had come in low across the ice, nipped, and slid away before Nocturne could turn.

Nocturne had stopped trying to turn quickly. She stood with her legs spread and the spikes of her hind heels driven down into the grey ice until they bit, and she let the Buizel come round her.

*Come closer. Behind me, where you think I'm blind.*

The scrape of claws went past her left flank and on round toward her hindquarters. Nocturne did not look. She heard the twin tails whirr as the Buizel checked her slide, heard the soft grunt of a small body gathering itself.

Her tail went heavy.

It began at the root and ran out to the tip, a weight and a hardness that turned the sickle into something forged. The white fur along it lay flat and took on a dull grey sheen, like a blade left out in the frost. She held it low and still, curled a little to the right.

The Buizel came in.

Nocturne pivoted on her heel spikes. They held in the ice, and her whole hindquarters swung round on them like a gate, and the steel-hard tail came with the swing, low and flat over the ice, the curved edge leading. It met the Buizel at the hip just as her feet left the ground.

== strike act
Spray hung in the gorge so thick that Nocturne could taste it on her teeth.

The falls came down behind the Buizel in a white wall and broke into the plunge pool with a roar that took every other sound away. The Buizel stood in the shallows at the pool's edge, up to her knees, soaked through, water running in sheets off her fins. She was resting. Her twin tails hung slack in the current. She thought she was safe there, with water all round her.

Nocturne had been saving it for this.

She could feel the charge where it always lived, a dry, tight crawl under the skin of her chest and along the roots of her ruff. It had been building since she woke, and it would not build again quickly. Spend it badly and it was gone.

*All of it. Once.*

She planted herself on the flat dry slab above the pool and let it rise. Her ruff lifted. Every hair on her neck and chest stood out stiff, and a faint blue tick ran along them, snapping. Her horn hummed. The fur along her spine rose in a ridge to the root of her sickle tail, and the ache of holding it all in grew sharp behind her teeth.

The Buizel looked up. Her black eyes found the bristling ruff and understood it, and she started to back toward the deep water.

Wrong direction.

Nocturne lowered her horn at the wet orange body and let the charge go. It left her in a single crooked line of light that cracked off the tip of the horn and jumped the gap to the pool, and the gorge went bright, and the stroke found the Buizel's chest.

== strike act
Ripples went under the falls and let the weight of them press her to the bottom of the pool.

It was dark and loud down there, a drumming she felt in her teeth, and cold enough to clear her head. She sat on the gravel with her cheeks puffed and her eyes open and looked up through the churning white at the shape on the rock shelf above. White. Pacing. Waiting for her to come up where she had gone down.

*Not there. Never there.*

She turned on the bottom, put her feet to a sunken stone and pushed off sideways, and let her twin tails take her. They spun up behind her until they hummed, and the pool slid by her like wind, and she swung round under the shelf in a wide arc, out of sight of the white shape, toward the far side where the rock came down low and flat into the water.

There she called the water to her. It came out of the pool in a skin, cold and tight, wrapping her from the crown of her head to the cream tips of her tails, and her fins folded flat to her forearms.

She broke the surface without slowing.

The Absol's head was turned the wrong way, toward the falls. Ripples saw the ruff, wet in spite of everything, the dark face in profile, the sickle tail held stiff for balance on the slick rock. She saw the near shoulder, the right one, and the gap beneath it where the foreleg met the chest.

She tucked her chin, put her own shoulder forward, and shot up out of the pool at it, water tearing off her in a hissing veil, and struck.

== strike act
The boulder was taller than she was, and Ripples climbed it quietly, claws and the flats of her feet, while the Absol was still watching the channel for her.

It had been a good dive. She had gone in loud, with a splash, and come out a long way down the cave in a shallow pool behind the stalagmites, and the white head was still turned toward the deep water, ears forward, horn ready for something that was not coming from there.

From the top of the boulder Ripples could see the whole ledge. The stripe of grey daylight. The algae shining on the low stone. The Absol on the dry grit in the middle of it all, broad paws set, sickle tail lifted a little off the ground.

*Look round. Go on.*

She wanted the face. A jet to the flank would only make that ruff bristle. In the face, in the eyes and the nose, it would buy her the moment she needed to get down off this rock and close.

She crouched, both feet gripping the cold stone, and drew the breath down deep. The cold gathered in her throat, a pressure behind her tongue, and she held it there and waited, cheeks tight.

The Absol's ear twitched. Her head began to come round, slowly, following some small sound, the scrape of a claw on the boulder perhaps, perhaps nothing.

Ripples let her see. She let her see all of it, the orange shape on the boulder top, the open mouth. Then she sent the jet down through the grey light, hard and narrow, straight at the dark face, and it struck squarely between the red eyes.

== strike act
Cold came easily out here. Ripples had only to open her mouth.

She stood on bare grey ice where the wind had scoured the snow away, her feet spread, her twin tails held out stiff behind her to keep her upright, and breathed in through her teeth. The air bit. She let it. She let the chill settle at the back of her jaw and gather there, and felt frost begin to prickle along her lips and coat her small fangs until they ached.

The Absol was a long stride off, crouched, horn low. Her breath smoked. She was not happy on the ice; Ripples had watched her place every paw as if the lake were made of eggshells, the heel spikes scratching at each step for something to hold.

*Can't jump on this. Can't turn on it either.*

So Ripples would make her try.

She feinted left. Just her shoulders and her head, a jerk, a hiss. The Absol shifted to meet it, and the near forepaw, the right, slid on the ice, and the white body tipped toward it to catch itself.

Ripples went right and low. She kicked off with both feet and let the ice do the rest, sliding on her belly under the swing of the horn, arms tucked, tails trailing. The white foreleg came at her, braced and planted, close enough to see the spike standing out at the elbow.

She came up onto her knees at the end of the slide and opened her jaws wide. Frost smoked off her fangs. She bit down into the foreleg just below that elbow spike.

== strike act
Ripples had stopped trying to go through the Absol. She went round the oak instead.

The trunk was broad and old, the bark deep-cut, and its roots stood up out of the grass like the backs of half-buried animals. She slipped between them on quick feet, ducking low, the shade cool on her back after the sun of the beach. On the far side the white body turned to follow her, and the scythe of the horn caught on a low branch and dragged leaves down.

*Big. Big is slow round corners.*

She came out on the side the Absol was not facing. There it was, broad and white and close: the haunch, the hind leg with its heel spike set in the grass, and behind it the sickle tail lashing, half raised. Too dangerous, the tail. She wanted the leg.

The Absol knew it a moment late. Her head swung round over her own shoulder, red eyes finding the orange shape at her flank, and the haunch began to turn away.

Ripples ducked under the turn, bent nearly double, and her twin tails flung out sideways to keep her from tipping over in the long grass. It brought her in close, closer than the Absol could reach with horn or claw. She opened her jaws as wide as they would go, lips pulled back from her sharp little teeth, and drove her head forward at the back of the thigh, just above the heel spike, where the fur was thinner and the muscle bunched to push.

Her teeth sank into the white fur.

== strike act
The storm was on her side, and Ripples meant to use it.

The clearing had gone to puddles. Rain hammered the grass flat, the pines at its edge roared and bowed in the wind, and every so often the sky lit up and the whole place stood out pale and shadowless. Ripples did not mind any of it. Rain was only water falling. She stood in it with her fur slicked dark and her black eyes half shut against the drops, and watched the Absol hate it.

The white fur was soaked through. The ruff hung in heavy ropes. Every time a gust drove rain into the dark face, the Absol shut her eyes for an instant, and her ears went back.

*Blink, then. Blink again.*

Ripples began to circle toward the wind, so that it was at her back and in the Absol's face. She kept low. Her feet found the wet ground carefully, and her twin tails swung behind her, balancing her on the slick grass.

The next gust came. It ripped water off the pines and flung it across the clearing in a sheet, and the red eyes closed.

Ripples went in under it. A few quick steps, splashing, and a jump at the end of them, and her right arm went back with the webbed paw open and the small claws spread. She aimed at the face, at the dark blue-gray muzzle just below the eyes, where she had seen the Absol wince before.

The red eyes opened. They saw her in the air, already arriving. Her claws raked across the dark muzzle.

== strike take light
The jet caught Nocturne on the left shoulder and burst into spray across her ruff.

It was not much of a blow. It shoved her a half-step sideways on the grass and stung, a cold slap that soaked through to the skin and spread there. What she minded was the wet. Water ran down inside her ruff and along her chest and dripped off her elbows, warm from the lake and smelling of weed, and her lip came up off her teeth in a long, silent hiss.

She shook the shoulder once, hard. Droplets flew off her in a fan and pattered on the fallen trunk beside her. The muscle under the fur ached a little, a dull bruised throb, and she rolled it as she stepped forward and it moved as it should.

*Cheap. She has nothing heavier, or she would have used it.*

The Buizel was already moving, scuttling sideways along the edge of the sand toward the shallows, cheeks still puffed from the shot. Her twin tails flicked behind her.

Nocturne did not chase her into the water. She went the other way, up onto the mossy trunk, and walked along it with her claws hooked into the soft green, where she could look down. The wood gave a little under her and held. From up there she could see the whole strip of beach and where it narrowed against the reeds, and she could see that the Buizel would have to come out of the shallows there, or not at all.

She stood and dripped and watched, and let the sting in her shoulder fade to nothing.

== strike take light
The small claws went across the back of her right foreleg, quick as a flick, and were gone.

Nocturne felt it as a thin line of heat just above the paw, bright and shallow, the way a bramble feels. She drew the leg in under her with a short huff through her nose and set it down again on the grit. It held her. The sting was already settling into an itch.

The Buizel had not stayed to see. She was off across the ledge on her quick feet, twin tails whirling behind her for balance, heading for the algae-slick stone and the pools beyond. Running to the water, as always.

*Claws and runs. She can't stay and fight on dry rock.*

Nocturne licked once at the scratch, tasted a little salt and a little blood, and let it be. Then she went after her, not fast, keeping to the dry. She did not need to be fast. The Buizel had to cross the green stone to reach the channel, and the green stone was treacherous even for feet made for it.

Halfway across, the orange body skidded. A foot went out. The Buizel caught herself on a stalagmite with both webbed paws and hung there an instant, scrabbling.

Nocturne stopped at the very edge of the dry grit, as close as she would go, and lowered her horn. The foreleg hardly troubled her. She put her weight on it to be sure and it said nothing at all.

The Buizel looked back over her shoulder, saw where she was, and her cheek marks twitched.

== strike take light
The charge came in low and took her across the front of the ruff, and on ice that was enough to move her.

Nocturne went back. She did not fall; her paws simply slid, all of them together, and she skated backward across the grey ice with the water from the jet streaming off her chest and stiffening in her fur. A grunt left her, more surprise than hurt. The thick white ruff had taken most of it. Under it her breastbone ached, flat and dull, as if she had walked into a branch in the dark.

Her heel spikes caught. She felt them scrape, catch, and bite, and the slide stopped with a jerk that she took in her hips.

*That is what the ruff is for.*

The Buizel had shot on past where she had been standing and was struggling to stop. Out of the water, the jet had nothing left to carry it; it broke into spray and ice around her, and she spun on her belly in a slow circle, arms out, twin tails slapping the ice as she tried to get her feet back under her.

Nocturne shook her ruff. Crusted droplets clicked together in it and broke loose. The ache under her chest was already shrinking, and she breathed into it once, deep, to measure it, and found nothing that would slow her.

She took a step. The ice held under her paws; she dug in the spikes and took another. The Buizel was getting up, slipping, getting up again, a long stride off, her back still half turned.

Nocturne lowered her head and went to meet her before she finished.

== strike take light
Small teeth closed on the back of Nocturne's left hind leg and held.

It was a pinch more than a bite, sharp and sudden, and it made her kick. The leg jerked out of the mud with a wet suck and the Buizel came with it, still hanging on, her twin tails thrashing at the reeds. Nocturne growled, low, through her shut teeth, and kicked again.

The teeth let go. The Buizel landed on her back in the shallow brown water with a slap and rolled clear.

*Like a rat in a ditch.*

Nocturne put the foot down. The bite throbbed, a small hot ache in the meat above the heel spike, and she could feel each little tooth-mark as its own spot of warmth. She leaned onto it. It took her weight without complaint. There would be a stiffness there later. There was none now worth the name.

She turned, slowly, because nothing in this place could be done quickly. The mud clung to her belly fur and weighed down the tip of her sickle tail. The Buizel was on her feet again among the reeds, a stride away, mud plastered up her orange sides and over the cream of her stomach, her black eyes bright and her mouth still open.

Pleased with herself. Nocturne could see it in the set of the small shoulders, in the quick turn of the tails.

She lowered her horn and did not give her the satisfaction of a limp. A step. Another. The mud sucked at each paw and let it go, and the bitten leg went down and came up with the others, steady.

== strike take light
Claws scraped down her raised forearm, across the blue fin, and Ripples snatched the arm back with a hiss.

It stung like a slapped nose. The fin folded flat on its own and stayed flat, smarting along its edge, and the orange fur below it was ruffled the wrong way in a row of lines. No more than that. She shook the arm out hard, flicking water off the webbed paw, and felt the smart draw down to a thin, bright ache that she could carry.

*Got the fin. Only the fin.*
