She was backing already, feet light on the wet stone. The Absol had overreached to land that blow. The white body was stretched long on the grit, a forepaw still out, and for the space of a breath she was off balance, weight too far forward.

Ripples saw it and could do nothing with it; she was too far back. She filed it away. Overreaches on the swing. Comes forward behind it.

Her heel found the edge of a shallow pool and she stopped there, ankle-deep, the cold soaking up into her feet. Better. Her twin tails swayed behind her. The fin had begun to lift again, slowly, as the hurt went out of it.

The Absol drew herself back together, paws under her, horn held straight out at her. The red eyes flicked to the pool around Ripples' feet and narrowed.

Ripples chittered at her, short and sharp, and spread her arms.

== strike take light
The steel tail only clipped her, and that was luck, not skill.

Ripples had been halfway into a sidestep when it came round, and the blade edge went past her stomach by a whisker while the flat of the hard sickle smacked against the point of her left hip. It shoved her sideways. Her feet skidded on the wet rock of the shelf, and her twin tails flew out wide and whirled to catch her, and she got a paw on a mossy boulder and held.

A grunt came out of her, short. Her hip throbbed. It was the deep, blunt kind of hurt that comes from knocking into a doorframe, and she knew it would be a bruise by evening and not much more.

*Heavy, though. Like being hit with a rock on a rope.*

Spray drifted over them both from the falls. She blinked it out of her eyes and pushed off the boulder, testing the leg: weight on it, weight off, weight on. It held. It complained a little. She told it to be quiet.

The Absol was still turning out of the swing, the grey sheen fading from her tail as it slowed. All that weight had to go somewhere when it missed, and it had carried her round, and her hindquarters were toward Ripples now, and her head was coming back too late.

Ripples did not waste time rubbing the hip. She bared her small teeth and went straight back in, low, before the horn could find her.

== strike take light
The jaws closed on one of her tails, near the cream tip, and Ripples yelped more from outrage than from pain.

The Absol did not bite down hard. It was a grab, a snatch at whatever trailed, and for a moment Ripples was held by it, her feet scrabbling in the soft pale sand, her whole rear end jerked back. The tail pinched and burned where the teeth sat. She twisted round on herself, chittering furiously, and swung her free tail at the dark face as hard as it would go.

It slapped across the Absol's eye. The jaws opened. Ripples tumbled forward onto her paws and knees and was up again in the same motion.

*She bit my tail. My tail.*

She whipped it round in front of her and looked at it. The cream tip was wet and ruffled, the fur pressed into a row of little dents, and it smarted when she flexed it. That was all. It still turned. Both of them still turned. She spun them behind her, just to be sure, and they whirred together as they should.

The Absol was blinking the slapped eye, head tilted, a low growl rolling in her chest. She did not look sorry.

Ripples backed toward the warm shallows, feet sinking in the sand with every step, and kept her tails tight behind her now, out of reach, close against the backs of her legs. Lesson learned. She showed the Absol her teeth and wondered what else on that white body was trailing.

== strike take light
Most of it went into the puddle.

The line of light jumped from the Absol's horn, crooked as a root, and Ripples was already throwing herself aside. It struck the ground where her feet had been, and the water there flashed and hissed, and the edge of it caught her trailing foot and ran up her leg into her body.

Her fur stood on end. All of it, from her ears to the tips of her tails, a whole-body bristle that made her look as round as a thistle head. A tingle raced through her, sharp in her teeth and the pads of her paws, and every muscle twitched at once. She landed in the wet grass in a heap and lay there for a heartbeat with her fins jerking.

Then it was over. Her leg buzzed like a foot that has fallen asleep. Her jaw ached where it had clenched.

*Missed. Mostly missed.*

She rolled onto her feet and shook herself, hard, and the fur lay down again, more or less, in a sodden, spiky mess. Rain hammered on her. Thunder rolled away over the pines.

The Absol had not followed. She stood across the clearing with her ruff still lifted and crackling faintly, her sides heaving, and Ripples saw her shut her mouth on a breath the way a creature does after lifting something too heavy.

Spent, maybe. Some of it.

Ripples flexed her buzzing foot, put it down in a puddle on purpose, and lifted her chin at the Absol through the rain.

== strike take mid
The dark claws went across her right side, over the ribs, and the cold of the lake was nothing to the cold they left behind.

Ripples felt them as a pull first, a drag through her fur, and then as lines of heat that sank straight in and spread under the skin. Her breath stopped. A short, sharp *ah* jumped out of her before she could shut her teeth on it, and she skidded sideways on the ice with her right arm clamped down over the place.

She stayed up. Just. Her feet went in different directions and her twin tails thrashed out behind her and caught her, and she came to a stop crouched and lopsided, side pulled in.

*Ribs. Of course the ribs.*

Breathing in was the problem. A shallow breath was bearable. A deep one dragged at the cuts and the bruise under them and made her whole right side clench, so she took shallow ones, quick, and felt how little air they brought.

She straightened, slowly. It pulled. She did it anyway, because the Absol was watching her from a long stride away with her claws still smoking faintly dark, and Ripples was not going to stand there bent over like something old.

The right arm stayed close to her ribs. She could lift it; she found that out when she tried, and found out also that she did not want to try again soon. She let it hang there, guarding, and put her weight on the left foot.

Slower now. She could feel it in herself. She would have to be clever instead.

Her black eyes went past the Absol to the dark crack in the ice, where open water showed.

== strike take mid
The teeth went into her left shoulder from behind and stayed there long enough for her to feel every one.

Ripples had not heard the Absol come through the reeds. She heard her now, a deep growl right against her back, and felt the jaws clamp and the weight behind them drive her down to her knees in the mud. A thin *yip* broke out of her. Her left arm jerked and then would not do as it was told.

The jaws let go. She was free, and the shoulder began to throb in earnest, deep and pulsing, as though the teeth were still in it. She scrambled away on her knees and her good paw, mud to the elbows, and turned with her back to a half-sunk stump.

*Stupid. Watched the front.*

The arm hung at her side. When she tried to raise it, the shoulder answered with a hard, bright stab that made her suck air through her teeth, and the fin on that forearm folded down flat and trembling. She stopped trying. She held the arm against her ribs with the other one and breathed.

The Absol stood a stride off, mud to her belly, lips peeled back, a few orange hairs caught between her teeth.

Ripples got her feet under her. She had to lean on the stump to do it, and the stump was slick, and the shoulder knocked at every jolt. She stood anyway. Her twin tails swept behind her, low, steadying her in the mud.

One arm, then. She still had teeth.

== knockdown take
It found her standing in the water, and the water carried it everywhere.

Ripples felt the crack before she heard it. The light struck her chest and went through her, out along her arms and down her legs into the pool around her knees, and then the pool gave it back from below. Every muscle she had locked at once. Her back arched. Her fins snapped wide and stiff. A short, strangled cry got out between her clenched teeth and was lost in the roar of the falls.

Then it let go of her, and she fell forward into the shallows on her webbed paws.

The water was cold on her face. She stayed there a moment on her paws and knees, head hanging, and could not make her arms stop trembling. They shook in fine, fast jerks, all on their own, and her jaw ached from the clench, and her chest where the stroke had landed felt scorched under the fur, tight and tender, as if she had lain too long on a sun-baked stone.

*Out of the water. It goes through the water.*

She got up. It took longer than it should. Her legs did what she asked, but late and stiffly, like legs that had been cold a long time, and on the first step toward the rocks she nearly fell.

She made the dry shelf and stood on it, swaying a little, arms held out from her sides because her fingers still twitched. The smell of singed fur hung in the spray.

Across the pool the Absol's ruff was lying down again, flat and spent. Ripples watched it and counted her own breaths and waited for her paws to come back to her.

== knockdown take
The sickle tail came down across the front of her right thigh like a branch falling, and the leg went out from under her.

Ripples hit the gritty stone on her side. The thigh had gone hot and heavy all at once, a deep, swelling ache that started where the steel edge had struck and spread up into her hip and down to the knee. She curled her toes and the whole leg answered with a throb. Her breath came out through her nose in a hard huff, and she bit down on the noise that wanted to follow.

*Up, before she comes round again.*

She rolled onto her good side and pushed. Arms first. Then the left leg under her. Then, carefully, the right, and when she put weight on it the thigh bunched and shook and a short squeak got out of her before she could stop it. She stood. She stood mostly on the left, the right foot set down light on its toes, like a bird with a sore claw.

The Absol had come round on the dry grit, tail grey and hard and swinging low, the sheen just beginning to go out of it.

Ripples took a step backward toward the pools. It was a bad step, a hitch and a hop, and she felt her twin tails jerk out sideways to keep her upright. She would not be quick on this leg. Not for a while.

So she would not go to the Absol. She would make the Absol come into the water, where a leg mattered less.

She backed another hitching step, and another, until the cold came up over her ankles.

== strike take mid
The teeth went in below her right elbow, and with the teeth came the cold.

Nocturne had braced for a bite. She had not braced for this. The small fangs sank into the meat of her foreleg and the cold went in after them, sharp and spreading, a deep aching chill that ran down to her paw and up into her shoulder and locked the muscle around it. She jerked her head down and hissed, a long rasp through her teeth, and swung the leg.

The Buizel let go and skidded away on her belly across the ice.

Nocturne set the paw down. It did not want to be set down. The leg below the elbow had gone stiff and dull and tight, frost glittering in the white fur where the teeth had been, and when she put weight on it the chill inside turned into a hard, gnawing ache that made her lip lift off her fangs.

*Her teeth. Watch her teeth, not her claws.*

She took her weight back onto the other legs. The bitten paw she rested on the ice with only its pads touching, and even so the cold of the lake came up through it and made the ache worse.

She could still fight on it. Not well. Swinging with that paw would cost her; landing on it after a lunge would cost more.

The Buizel was up again, a long stride away, spitting flecks of frost and grinning. Nocturne turned so the hurt leg was away from her, horn forward, and growled low enough that the ice seemed to carry it.

== knockdown take
The water took her in the ribs and lifted her clean off her paws.

Nocturne had seen the Buizel's cheeks fill and had thought it would be another little jet. It was not. It hit her left side like the whole lake leaning on her at once, a thick, roaring column that knocked the breath out of her and threw her across the grass. She landed on her right side among the roots of the old oak, a root catching her under the shoulder, and the water kept pouring over her for a breath longer before it broke.

She lay there gasping. A hiss came out of her, then a low, broken growl.

Her ribs on the left had gone tight and hot under the soaked fur, one broad bruised ache from her elbow to her hip. When she breathed in deeply, it bit. She learned that at once and stopped breathing deeply.

*Not a jet. She's been saving that.*

She got her paws under her, then her haunches, then her head, water streaming out of her ruff and off her horn and down her face, and the ache in her side pulled at every movement. She stood with her left side turned a little away and her elbow tucked in to guard it.

Wet. Wet through, to the skin, the warm weedy smell of the lake in her nose. Her lip curled. That, more than the ribs, made her want to tear something.

The Buizel stood at the edge of the sand with her mouth still open and her chest heaving, spent from the effort. Nocturne could see the cost of it in her.

She walked toward her, slower than she wanted to, favouring the side.

== strike take mid
The rush came up out of the pool and took her under the jaw.

Nocturne's head snapped back. Her teeth clacked together on the tip of her tongue, and the small, hard body in its skin of water drove on through, into her throat and the front of her ruff, and shoved her backward along the wet rock shelf until her hindquarters met the cliff.

She tasted blood. A short, choked growl came out of her, and her jaw hung open a moment while the ache in it gathered.

It gathered quickly. The whole underside of her face throbbed, from the chin back to the hinge below each ear, and when she closed her mouth the teeth met wrong for an instant and the hinge sent a hard jolt up into her skull. She shut her eyes against it. Opened them.

*Under the chin. She came from below.*

The Buizel had bounced off her and landed in the shallows, splashing, and was already scrambling round to face her.

Nocturne swallowed. It hurt. She worked her jaw once, slowly, side to side, and it moved, stiff and sore and swollen-feeling, but it moved. Biting would be hard for a while. Holding anything in her teeth would be worse.

She pushed off the cliff and stood square on the shelf with the spray drifting round her and her mouth a little open, breathing through it. She kept her chin tucked in against her ruff now, low, and her horn forward.

The Buizel's black eyes went to her jaw. Nocturne saw her notice, and her ears went flat.

== strike take mid
The claws caught her across the nose, and for a moment there was nothing in the swamp but that.

It was a small cut. Nocturne knew it even as her eyes filled. But the nose was the one soft place on her face, and the Buizel's claws had dragged right across the tender grey skin, and the sting went straight up behind her eyes and made them stream. A sharp *hss* burst out of her. She shook her head hard, horn swinging, and mud flew off her ruff.

Her nose throbbed. It went hot and full, beating with her heart, and the smell of the swamp was suddenly very far away behind it. She could taste a little blood at the back of her mouth.

*Little clawing beast.*

She backed a step to clear her eyes, and the mud sucked at her hind paws and held them, and she had to wrench them free. Her eyes watered. Through it she could see an orange smear dancing at the edge of the reeds, quick and low.

She blinked hard. The world came back, mostly. Her nose did not stop. Every breath she pulled through it rasped on the way in and made the throb jump, so she opened her mouth and breathed through that instead, panting a little, and hated the sound.

The Buizel was coming again. Nocturne lowered her head to meet her, and lowering it sent the blood pounding into her nose and made her eyes sting afresh, and she did it anyway and held it there.

== dodge
Ripples saw the tail go grey and knew what was coming.

She had seen that sheen before. The white fur along the sickle flattened and hardened and took on the dull shine of cold metal, and the Absol's hindquarters bunched and began to swing, and there was nowhere on this bare ice to step that the tail would not reach.

So she did not step. She fell.

She threw her feet out from under her and went down flat on her back, arms tucked to her chest, chin in, and slid. The lake was glass under her. It carried her the way water would, and she went skating along beneath the swing with the steel tail passing over her face close enough that its wind lifted the fur on her cheeks. She heard it whistle. She heard it hit nothing.

*Ha. Too high.*

The ice spun her slowly. She saw sky, then the white flank, then the far shore with its frozen reeds. She let her twin tails drag behind her as a brake, and they scraped and caught and turned her, and she came to a stop on her belly a long stride beyond the Absol's hind legs.

The Absol had gone round with her own swing. All that weight in the tail had nowhere to stop, and it carried her half about, claws scratching, heel spikes skidding as she fought to plant them.

Ripples got her knees under her. Her back was cold and wet where the ice had been. She stayed low, crouched, watching the tail lose its sheen, and waited for the Absol to find her.

== dodge
Nocturne heard the Buizel draw breath before she saw her do it.

It was a big breath, deep and long, louder than the rain, and the small orange chest swelled with it. Nocturne knew that sound now. She had been hit by what came after it once already.

*Not again. Not straight on.*

She did not run from it. Running in a straight line on this wet grass would only give the water something to follow. She went sideways instead, a long bounding step to her left, toward the nearest pine, and her paws skidded on the flattened grass as she landed, and she let them skid, let the slide carry her round behind the trunk.

The water came. It crossed the clearing in a thick white roar and struck the pine where her flank had been, and the whole tree shook. Bark burst off it. Needles came down on her in a shower of cold drops, and the spray from the column swept round both sides of the trunk and soaked what little of her was still dry.

She stood flat against the bark with her ears back and her teeth bared, wet to the skin and untouched.

The roar stopped. Behind it there was only rain again, and somewhere out in the clearing, a small sound of ragged breathing.

Nocturne put her head round the trunk. The Buizel stood in the open grass with her mouth still open and her shoulders heaving, her twin tails slack behind her, emptied out.

Nocturne came out from behind the pine at a run.

== dodge
The dark claws came down where Ripples' head had been, and Ripples was already in the air.

She had read them in the Absol's shoulders. The weight had gone onto the hind legs and the white chest had risen and the right forepaw had drawn back, its claws smoking with shadow, and the wet rock under Ripples' feet had told her there was only one way out. Back. Off the shelf.

She jumped backward with her arms thrown wide. The claws passed through the spray in front of her face. A shadowed tip snagged a few hairs off the cream spot above her eye, and that was all.

Then she was falling, and then the pool had her.

It closed over her with a deep, cold boom. She went down in a cloud of bubbles, and the noise of the falls became a drum, and her twin tails began to spin without being asked, and the water held her up and turned her and gave her all her speed back at once. She surfaced in the middle of the pool, a long way from the shelf, blinking.

*Mine now. Come in and get me.*

The Absol stood on the lip of the rock where she had been. The dark had gone out of her claws. She looked down at the green water swirling below, then at Ripples, and her lip lifted, and she did not move a single paw nearer the edge.

Ripples floated with only her eyes and the top of her head above the surface and let her tails turn lazily beneath her, waiting.

== throw
The Buizel had come in too close, and Nocturne let her.

She had been clinging to Nocturne's ruff with both paws, trying to get her teeth into the side of the white neck, her feet kicking at the chest, her twin tails lashing for balance. It was a nuisance. It was also, Nocturne decided, an opportunity: everything that small body weighed was hanging off the front of her.

*Hold on, then. Hold on tight.*

She dropped her head and shoved it in under the Buizel's arms, the flat of her skull against the cream stomach, the horn turned well aside so its edge would not catch. The small paws clawed at her ruff. The teeth snapped at her ear and missed.

Nocturne set her hind legs in the soft earth at the edge of the grass, heel spikes biting, and straightened her whole body at once.

She threw from the haunches. The power went up through her back and her neck and into the lift of her head, and the Buizel came off her ruff with a squeak, tearing out a few white hairs, and went up and over. Nocturne turned her head as she lifted, aiming. Behind her, across the strip of grass, the fallen trunk lay along the edge of the pines, furred with moss, rotting soft at one end and hard as stone at the other.

She aimed for the hard end.

The weight left her at the top of the heave. She watched the orange shape turn over in the sunlight, tails whirling uselessly, against the blue of the sky.

== throw
Ripples could not lift the Absol. She had known that from the start. She did not need to.

The white body was coming at her in a lunge, low and fast, horn first, every paw pushing at the ice. Too fast for the ice. Ripples could see the paws skating under it, the heel spikes scratching for a grip they were not finding. The Absol was committed. All her weight was going forward, and none of it could stop.

*Then I'll help her keep going.*

Ripples stepped aside at the last moment, a quick half-turn on her heel, and as the horn passed her chest she whipped both tails round the nearer foreleg, just above the paw. They wound round and round, cream tips crossing. She leaned back against them and planted her feet.

She did not pull the Absol toward her. She pulled the leg sideways, out from under its owner, at the instant it reached for the ice.

The leg went. The Absol went after it. Her own lunge carried her on and over, the bound foreleg yanked away to the side, and the big white body pivoted round Ripples like a door on its hinge, sliding, turning, legs flailing, ruff flying out.

Ripples let go at the end of the turn. Her tails uncoiled with a snap and the whole weight of the Absol went skidding away across the ice on its side, faster than it had come, toward the place where the lake had buckled in the cold and thrown up a jagged ridge of grey slabs.

She had a moment to watch it go. Her tails ached at the roots from the strain. She grinned anyway.

== throw take
Ripples was in the air for longer than she liked.

The gorge went round her, wet black cliff and white water and grey sky, and her twin tails spun wildly behind her and found nothing to push against. She tucked. It was all she had time to do.

The mossy boulder at the edge of the pool took her across the back.

The moss was thin, and the rock under it was not. The blow knocked the air out of her in a hard squeak and drove her shoulders forward round her chest, and the back of her head knocked the stone in the same instant, a dull clop that made her teeth click and lit small sparks behind her eyes.

Then she slid off it. The rough face of the boulder dragged down her spine and her tails, and she dropped the last little way into the shallows at its foot, landing on her rump in the cold water with a splash that went up over her ears.

She sat there. Water ran out of her fur. Her back ached from the shoulder blades to the root of her tails in one broad, deep bruise, and the bump on her skull throbbed in time with it.

*Up, before the white thing comes down off the rock.*

Her arms worked. She leaned on them and pushed, and her back complained at the bend, and she stopped halfway and stayed hunched there in the shallows, breathing, letting the water cool her.

The Absol was picking her way down the wet rock toward her, slow and careful on the slick stone, hating every step that brought her nearer the pool.

Ripples watched her come and spat out a mouthful of the pool.

== throw take
Nocturne went across the ice on her side, legs scrabbling, and could not stop.

There was nothing to stop with. Her claws scored pale lines in the grey and found no purchase. Her heel spikes caught and skipped and caught again, jarring her hips with every bite, and the lake turned slowly round her as she slid: the shore, the sky, the small orange shape standing in the middle of the ice with its tails uncoiling, the shore once more.

Then the pressure ridge.

It was a wall of broken slabs where the ice had heaved up on itself in the cold, knee-high and jagged, edged like flint. She hit it with her back. The flat of the biggest slab took her across the spine between the shoulder blades, and a jagged edge beside it raked the back of her right hind leg in the same instant, and she stopped dead with a grunt that came out of her like a cough.

Fragments of ice rained down on her. They pattered off her ruff and slid into her fur, cold and wet against the skin.

*On my back. On the ice. In front of her.*

She lay there a breath with her legs in the air, the slabs pressing into her shoulders, her spine aching in a hard bar across the top of her back. The scraped leg stung along its whole length.

She hated it more than the hurt: lying belly-up in the cold with the Buizel watching. Her lip lifted. A low growl came out of her, not at the pain, at the sky.

She rolled, got her paws against the ridge, and began to push herself upright.

== pin hold
Ripples had never been on top of anything this big, and it was very hard to stay there.

The Absol lay on her side in the mud with her head half sunk in it, and Ripples knelt on the white neck just behind the horn, both knees in the wet ruff, her whole small weight pressing down. Her twin tails were wound tight round the forelegs, binding them together above the elbow spikes, cinched as hard as she could make them. Her webbed paws were spread on the dark face, one on the cheek and one at the base of the horn, holding the head down into the brown.

*Don't let the head up. If the head comes up, everything comes up.*

Under her knees she could feel the Absol breathe. Big slow breaths, much bigger than her own, and each one lifted her a little and set her down again. The muscles under the fur were not slack. They hummed. Every so often something in the long white body tested her, a shift of the haunches, a pull against the tails, and each time she leaned harder and felt it settle.

Her tails ached. They were not made for this, for holding; they burned at the roots and trembled along their length, and she knew they would not hold forever.

The red eye nearest her rolled up and found her face. It did not blink. Mud had splashed across the dark cheek beneath it.

Ripples looked back down into it and pressed the horn deeper into the mud, and set her teeth, and did not let go.

== pin hold
The ice was doing half the work, and Nocturne let it.

The Buizel lay on her back on the bare grey lake, and the cold was coming up through her fur into the small orange body, making it slow. Nocturne stood over her. Both forepaws were planted on the narrow shoulders, pads spread, claws curled in so they pressed without cutting. Her hind legs were braced wide behind, heel spikes driven into the ice so that nothing under her could make her slide.

*Let her get cold. Cold things are slow.*

She kept her chest up, off the body. She did not need to lie on it. The shoulders were enough; with them held down, the arms could only paw at her elbows, and they did, now and then, the webbed paws scratching weakly at the fur of her forelegs and sliding off.

Her own forelegs ached from holding stiff. The left one, bitten earlier, throbbed dully at every push from below. She did not shift off it. She put her weight a little more onto it instead, so that the Buizel would feel no difference between one side and the other.

Below her the small face was turned up. Frost had begun to form on the whiskers and along the black marks of the cheeks. The breath came out in quick white puffs, each one smaller than the last.

The twin tails were what she watched. They lay out on the ice beyond the Buizel's head, twitching. More than once they had curled as if to whip round her. Each time she had pressed down on the shoulders, and they had uncurled.

They twitched again. Nocturne leaned, and they lay still.

== pin hold
The sand took the Buizel's shape as Nocturne pressed her into it.

It was soft here at the water's edge, pale and fine, and the small body sank into it under her weight: the shoulders, the hips, the twin tails buried to the tips somewhere underneath. Warm shallows lapped at Nocturne's hind paws. She hated that. She kept her hindquarters as far up the beach as she could and leaned forward to make up for it.

Her chest lay across the Buizel's stomach. Her right forepaw held the left arm flat to the sand by the wrist, just above the webbed paw, and her left forepaw lay on the cream throat, not pressing, only resting there, a reminder.

*Struggle and the sand fills in round you. Lie still and it holds you anyway.*

Under her chest the stomach moved fast. She could feel the Buizel's heart going like a bird's against her ruff. The free arm, the right one, pushed at her shoulder with the heel of its paw, steady, stubborn, getting nowhere.

The sun lay hot on her back. Flies hummed over the reeds. A small wave came in, wetted her heels, and went out again, taking a little of the sand from under the Buizel's head with it.

Nocturne noticed that. She shifted her forepaw a little higher on the throat, toward the chin, so that if the next wave took more, the head would not be able to turn.

The black eyes watched her do it. They did not look afraid. They looked as though they were thinking.

== pin struggle
Ripples could not move her hips, so she went for the leg.

The Absol had her flat in the wet grass, white chest crushed down on her ribs, a forepaw planted across her upper arm and the other on her hip. Rain beat on both of them. It ran into Ripples' eyes and her open mouth and she blinked it away, and above her the dark face was turned aside, out of the weather, watching her free arm.

The foreleg on her arm was close. Close enough. The white fur of it was right beside her cheek.

*Teeth, then. She forgot I have teeth.*

She twisted her neck as far as it would go and snapped at the foreleg just above the paw.

She got fur. Only fur. The Absol had felt the twist coming through the body beneath her and lifted the paw a hair, and the small teeth closed on a mouthful of wet white hair and nothing behind it. Before she could let go and try again, the paw came down. It came down on her shoulder this time, heavier, with the Absol's weight rocking forward behind it, and pressed her arm and shoulder into the mud under the grass.

The bruise in her shoulder flared. A thin squeak got out of her.

She spat the white hair out. She bucked, hard, and the chest on her ribs did not even rock. She tried to get a foot under her and found there was no room. Her twin tails thrashed in the grass beneath her and got nothing.

Rain hammered down. Ripples lay with her arm pinned and her ribs squeezed and stared up through it at the narrowed red eyes, breathing hard, and could think of nothing else to do yet.

== pin struggle
The tails would not give.

Nocturne lay on her side on the wet rock of the shelf, with spray drifting over her and the falls roaring, and the Buizel's twin tails were cinched round both her forelegs above the elbow spikes, binding them together. The small body crouched on her neck behind the horn. Webbed paws pressed down on her cheek.

*She weighs nothing. Nothing. Move.*

She gathered herself. Hindquarters first: she dug her heel spikes into the slick rock and shoved, trying to roll onto her belly and get her bound legs under her. The spikes skidded on the wet stone. Her hips came round a hand's width and slid back.

She tried the forelegs instead. She pulled them apart against the tails, hard, every muscle in her shoulders standing out under the fur. The tails stretched. They trembled. They held, and the pull of them bit into her legs just above the spikes, and the Buizel on her neck chittered and leaned harder.

Nocturne snapped her head up. The horn swung. But the small body was behind it, not in front, and the swing only threw the Buizel a little to the side before she clung on again with every limb she had, and the head went back down onto the rock, cheek first, with a knock that made her ear ring.

She lay still a moment, breathing hard through her teeth. Water ran across the rock under her face. Her shoulders ached from the pull.

The spray kept coming. The tails stayed tight. Above her she could hear the Buizel panting, fast and high, and that was the only comfort she had: it was costing her something too.

== pin struggle
The channel was right there. Ripples could hear it.

It lapped against the stone a long stride behind her head, deep and dark and cold, and if she could get to it she would be gone. Between her and it was the Absol. The white body lay heavy across her hips and stomach, pressing her into the shallow pool, and a broad forepaw was flat on her chest.

She wriggled. She did it slowly at first, a shift of the shoulders, a push of the heels against the slick stone under the water, inching herself upward, toward the channel, out from under. The algae helped. It was slippery as soap, and her back slid on it.

She moved a whisker. Another.

*Keep going. She hasn't noticed.*

The Absol had noticed. Ripples felt the weight on her hips shift, and the hind paws find the stone beyond her feet, and the whole white body came forward with her, smoothly, as if they had planned it together. The paw on her chest slid up to her collarbone and pressed. The chest followed it down onto her stomach.

She had gained nothing. Worse: she had lost the room she had had at her hips, and her breath was shorter now under the heavier weight.

She tried again, hard, all at once, heels and shoulders and both tails slapping the water under her. The algae let her slip a finger's width. The Absol's weight came after her and pinned her there.

Ripples lay back in the shallow pool, panting, and listened to the channel lap against the stone.

== getup
Nocturne's first try at standing put her straight back down on her chin.

The ice was the trouble. She had got her forepaws under her and pushed, and both of them had slid apart on the glassy grey surface, and her chest had dropped back onto the lake with a thump that knocked a cough out of her. Her back ached where she had landed. Her horn scraped the ice.

*Not like that. Not like a fool.*

She lay still and thought. Then she drew her hind legs up under her, slowly, and drove the spikes on her heels down into the ice until she felt them bite. They held. She rocked her weight back onto them and let her haunches come up first, the way she would rise from deep snow, with her forelegs still flat on the ice in front of her, taking nothing.

Only then did she bring the forepaws in, close together beneath her chest, claws spread. She pushed up with her hind legs doing most of it. Her front came off the ice. Her elbows locked. The ache in her back flared at the change and she let it, and stood.

She stood with her legs braced wide and her head low, a little unsteady, the heel spikes still dug in. Her ruff was full of ice crumbs. Her breath came out in short white bursts.

The Buizel was watching from across the ice. She had not come closer. Nocturne lifted her head, slowly, and shook the ice out of her ruff, and pointed her horn at her.

== getup
The mud did not want to let her go.

Ripples had come down on her back in it, deep, and when she tried to sit up it held her shoulders with a long, wet suck and pulled her straight back down. She lay there, blinking brown water out of her eyes. Her hip ached from the fall. Her tails were somewhere underneath her, stuck fast.

*Don't fight it. Fighting it is how you sink.*

She rolled instead. It was slow; the mud came with her in a great sticky sheet and fell off her side in clumps. She got onto her stomach, then onto her knees, mud to her elbows and dripping from her chin. Her twin tails came up out of it last, heavy, plastered to her legs, and she swung them hard to shake them free, and a spatter of brown flew off them across the reeds.

A drowned log lay within reach. She hooked her webbed paws over it, and its bark was slimy and soft, but under the slime the wood was solid. She pulled. Her hip complained. Her knees came up out of the muck with a slurp, and she got a foot flat on the log's edge and pushed herself the rest of the way upright.

She stood on the log, swaying, orange fur gone brown to the chest, cream stomach hidden under a crust of it. It was not dignified. It was standing.

The Absol was wading toward her through the reeds, slow and filthy and furious. Ripples flicked the mud from her fins and showed her teeth.

== dive
Valor climbed on four great beats of her blue wings, screaming the whole way, and turned over at the top as if she had hit a ceiling.

She did not circle. She did not wait. She simply folded and fell, crest flat, the red and white feathers of her tail pressed into a point, her yellow eyes fixed on the Absol below.

Nocturne saw her coming. It made no difference. The eagle flared at the last instant, wings booming open, and her talons came forward like a pair of grappling hooks. They struck the Absol's shoulders and the full weight of the fall drove her flat into the grass.

Valor beat once, twice, and tore free, a white tuft still caught in one talon. She hung above the clearing, chest heaving, and screamed down at her again.

*Get up. Get up so I can do it again.*

== dive take
Ripples heard the air tear above her and threw herself sideways, and it was not far enough.

Zephyr's dive clipped her across the back of the neck and shoulder, a blow like a branch swung by a giant, and the force of it spun her round and dropped her to one knee in the sand. Her fins flared wide on their own. The world rang.

By the time she looked up the Pidgeot was already climbing away, the long red and yellow crest streaming behind her, unhurried, as if she had all day.

*Can't fight what I can't reach.*

Ripples spat sand, swallowed the throb in her neck, and drew in a breath that tasted of lake water. Her cheeks puffed. If the bird wanted to stay up there, then the water would have to go up there after her.

== strike act
Cinder came across the clearing on her heavy legs, wings half open for balance, the flame on her tail burning high and white with eagerness.

Ripples tried to slip inside her reach. Cinder had been waiting for exactly that. She turned with it, and her clawed hand came round in a short hard hook that caught the Buizel under the ribs and lifted her half off her feet.

She followed it before Ripples could land: a breath drawn deep, smoke curling from her nostrils, and then the fire, a roaring orange tongue that rolled over the smaller fighter and set the grass at her feet smoking.

Cinder grinned through it, all small fangs. Her wings flared wide.

*Come on. Hit me back. Make it interesting.*

== strike take
The water hit Cinder square in the chest.

It was not the force of it, though there was force enough to rock her back on her heels. It was the cold, and the wet, and the sound her tail flame made: a furious hiss, a cloud of steam boiling off it, the flame shrinking to a spitting orange tongue.

She roared, more fury than pain, and swatted the spray aside with one wing. Steam rose off her scales all down her front. Her chest stung where the water had struck, a hot, wrong, tight feeling, like a burn turned inside out.

*Keep it away from the tail. Keep it away from the TAIL.*

She curled the tail in close behind her body and came on again, slower now, watching the Buizel's cheeks for the next breath.

== knockdown take
The bolt of dark energy caught Swift in the middle of her climb.

One moment she was rising, forked tail spread, already turning for the next pass. The next, something black and ringing hit her under the wing and the air simply stopped holding her. She tumbled once, wings flailing, saw ground, sky, ground again, and hit the grass on her side with a crack that knocked a burst of blue feathers loose.

She lay there for a moment with one wing spread wide in the dirt and the other folded wrong under her, beak open, chest heaving, not yet sure which way was up.

*Up. Get UP. You can't fight from down here.*

She dragged the folded wing free and it answered, sluggish and burning. She hopped once, twice, and could not quite make herself jump.

== strike act
Talon came out of the sun with her talons open.

She hit the Buizel across the shoulders, closed both feet, and beat her wings once, twice, and the ground let go of them. Ripples kicked at the air. The trees dropped away beneath them, then the pond, small and bright as a coin, and for a long moment at the top of the climb neither of them moved at all.

Then Talon opened her talons.

Ripples fell. She had time to twist once, paws out, looking for a ground that was still far too far away, and Talon was already folding her wings and dropping after her. The first slash caught her halfway down and spun her. The second came from directly above, the whole weight of the dive behind it, and drove her into the ground so hard the earth jumped in a ring around her.

She did not bounce. She lay in the shallow hollow her body had made, mouth open, no air in her at all.

*Again,* Talon thought, already climbing. *She'll get up. Do it again.*

== read
Ripples lay where the blow had left her and made herself look up.

Cinder hung above the clearing on slow, heavy wingbeats, not hurrying. She did not need to hurry. The left eye was swollen nearly shut, and every few beats the Charizard tipped her head a fraction to the right to keep the good one on her: a habit already, after one bad blow. Her chest moved deep and even. Too even. Ripples watched it and counted, and on the fifth breath it caught, a small hitch, smoothed away at once.

*There. You're tired too. You're just hiding it better.*

The good eye was not on her face. It was on her hip, the right one, the one that would not take her weight, and it rested there with a kind of satisfaction that made Ripples' fur rise along her spine. She knew that look. It was the look of someone deciding where to put the next one.

Her own hip throbbed under it, as if it knew it was being looked at. She held still and let it. Let her think the hip was all there was.

The wingbeat faltered once, the left wing a shade late. Cinder corrected it without seeming to notice. Ripples noticed.

== collapse
Ripples did not see it coming, because nothing was coming.

Cinder had pulled back to the edge of the clearing to breathe, and for a moment there was only the lake and the birds and the ache. Ripples stood in it. She had been standing for a while now on legs that had stopped reporting anything useful, and she was thinking about the next step, the one toward the dragon, when she noticed that her right knee had not come with her.

She looked down at it. It was bent. She had not bent it.

Then the left one went too, slowly, almost politely, and the grass rose to meet her shoulder. She lay on her side in it and stared at a stem a finger's length from her nose, a small green thing with an ant on it, and tried to work out how she had got there.

*Get up.*

Her paw moved against the ground and did not push. Her tails twitched once behind her and lay still. Somewhere above her she heard the dragon's breathing change, quickening, as Cinder saw.

== knockout
The second blast caught her before the first had finished with her.

Ripples felt it arrive in her chest and then she felt nothing at all. Cinder saw it happen: the Buizel's eyes, which had been fixed on her with that stubborn black glare for the whole fight, went somewhere else. They did not close. They simply stopped looking.

Then every part of her let go together. The fins went slack, the twin tails dropped, and she folded onto the wet sand with no attempt to catch herself, one arm under her at an angle she would never have allowed awake. She bounced once, a little, and lay still.

Cinder hung in the air above her and waited for the trick. The Buizel's side rose, slow and shallow. Fell. Rose again. Nothing else moved.

*That's it,* she thought, and found she did not quite believe it. *That's it.*

Her own wings were shaking. She let herself come down onto the sand, heavily, and stood there breathing in the sudden enormous quiet, looking at the small orange body that had cost her so much.

# ===============================================================================================================
# PASSAGES FOR ANY PAIR (build 148): {A} acts, {B} is acted on; shown in every fight as Wren and Sorrel.
# ===============================================================================================================

== super effective act
The heat came off {B} in waves, and the air around her shook with it. Every time she breathed out, the flame at the end of her tail flared high and bright, and the ground under her had gone pale and dry in a ring.

{A} had felt that heat on her face twice now. She did not want to feel it a third time.

She drew the water up from deep in her chest, cold and heavy, and held it there while {B} came round to her left, still blazing, still sure of herself. *Come closer. Come right up close.* {B} did. She dropped her head to rush, and the flame on her tail swung up behind her like a torch lifted in a gale.

{A} let the water go.

It left her in a hard, solid rope and struck {B} full across the chest and the side of the neck, and the sound it made was not the slap of water on skin. It was a long, furious *hssss*, and steam burst off {B} in a white cloud so thick that for a moment {A} lost sight of her altogether.

When the cloud tore open, {B} had stopped short in the middle of her rush. Her whole front was dark and dripping. The heat that had rolled off her a heartbeat ago was simply gone, and in its place a wet chill hung in the air between them. The tail flame had shrunk to a low, sputtering tongue, blue at the root, and it bent and shivered as though a wind were trying to pinch it out.

{A} watched it struggle. She watched {B} feel it struggle: the sudden stillness in her, the way her head turned, very slightly, toward her own tail before she could stop it.

*That is what frightens you. Not me. That.*

She heard it before she felt it. A deep, round *clang* that went out across the ground and came back off the rocks. Her feet slid on the gravel half a step. Her head rocked. Inside the plate where the blow had landed, a dull jar ran through her, spreading outward across her side and her shoulder, thinning as it went, until by the time it reached her legs it was hardly anything at all.

There was an ache. She admitted that much. A deep, flat soreness under the plate where it had struck, the kind that would stiffen later. She shifted her weight and felt it there, and it did not get worse.

*That's it. That's all of it.*

She looked at {A}.

{A} had come away from the blow worse than {B} had. That much was plain. She was shaking out her shoulder and her face was tight, and she was rubbing at the joint as if she could push the hurt back out of it. {B} watched her do it with a cool, steady interest.

They always did this. They always hit her as if she were made of the same soft stuff as they were, and the metal took it, and spread it, and gave most of it back to them. She had been built for exactly this. Her plates had held against stone and claw and teeth and they would hold against fists.

She rolled her shoulder once, slowly, to show {A} that she could.

The ache under the plate settled to a quiet throb. She could live with it. She could live with a great many more just like it.

*Hit me again. Ring me like a bell. See which of us gets tired first.*

She stepped forward, unhurried, and her feet came down heavy and sure on the gravel, and {A} stepped back.

== resisted take
The shadow came across the ground at {B} in a cold, bending ring, and she did not even brace for it.

It hit her chest and she felt it, a little: a chill that went through her and settled on her skin, a heaviness in her shoulders as though a wet cloak had been dropped on them, a dimming of the edges of the world. For a heartbeat her ears rang with a low, hollow hum.

Then it slid off her.

She felt it go, and almost smiled. The dark had come looking for somewhere to bite and found only more dark, and it had nothing to say to that. It poured off her shoulders, down her legs, pooled at her feet in a cold black puddle of shade, and seeped away into the ground and was gone. The chill on her skin went with it. Only a dull ache stayed behind in her chest, low and faint, where the push of the thing had landed.

*Mine. That's mine. You threw mine at me.*

She shook herself once, from neck to tail, the way one shakes off mist.

Across the ground, {A} stood with her head still lowered from the throw, and {B} watched her understand. It was not much to watch. A stillness. A slight lift of the head. But {B} knew that stillness. She made it herself, when a thing she trusted came back to her empty.

Something in her was almost sorry for it. She let that go. It had no use here.

She stretched her neck and felt the ache in her chest loosen and fade. Her breath came easy. The shadows round the edges of the ground felt close and friendly, the way they always did, and she could have reached out and pulled them round her shoulders like a coat.

*You can't frighten the dark with the dark.*

{B} lifted her lip from her teeth, slowly, and took a step toward {A}, and the shade went with her.

== opening
The clearing was quiet in the way of places that have been waiting a long time. A single tree stood near its middle, old and leaning, its roots knotted up out of the packed earth, and the wind moving through its upper leaves made a sound like slow breathing. Beyond it the ground ran down to the water's edge, where the grass gave way to grey stones and the stones gave way to a flat, still shine.

{A} came out of the long grass on the near side and stopped where it thinned. She stood there a while, letting her breath settle, looking at nothing in particular and at everything. The ground under her was dry and firm. She tested it with her weight, shifting once from one side to the other, and it held without a sound.

*Good footing. Remember where it ends.*

{B} was already there. She had been there, it seemed, for some time: in the shade under the tree, so still that the eye slid past her at first and had to come back. Now she moved, unhurried, out into the light. She did not look at the ground. She looked at {A}, steadily, from head to feet and back again, the way someone reads a letter twice to be sure of it.

Neither of them spoke. A bird somewhere in the branches gave a short, startled note and went silent.

{A} lowered her head a little. The air between them seemed to tighten, the way it does before rain, and she felt her own pulse in her throat, quick and even. Her body knew what this was before she had decided anything. Every part of her was listening.

*She's calm. Too calm. Let's see how long that lasts.*

{B} took a single step to the side, circling, keeping the tree at her back. {A} turned to follow, keeping her distance exactly. The leaves overhead stirred and settled. A dry stem cracked under someone's weight, and neither of them looked down to see whose.

The wind dropped. For the length of a held breath the whole clearing was still.

Then {A} moved.

== opening
Rain had fallen in the night, and the low ground beside the water still held it. Puddles lay in every hollow, flat and grey, each one holding a small piece of the sky. The rocks along the shore were dark and wet and smelled of moss. Further up, where the ground rose toward a line of boulders, it had already begun to dry, pale patches spreading through the mud like islands.

{B} stood up there on the drier ground. She had chosen it on purpose, that much was plain; she had placed herself with her back to the largest of the boulders and the open slope in front of her, so that anyone coming had to come uphill. She waited with her weight settled and her head high, and the cool morning air moved over her and she let it.

{A} came along the shore, unhurried, stepping round the deeper puddles without looking at them. She stopped at the foot of the slope. From there she could see all of it: the boulders, the drying mud, the rival on top of it all with the stone behind her.

*She thinks the hill is worth something. Maybe it is.*

She did not climb yet. She let her eyes go over {B} slowly, the set of her, the stillness, the small shift of weight that came when {A} stopped moving. Rested. Watchful. Not as calm as she was trying to look; there was a tightness about her breathing, a little too even, a little too careful.

{B} looked back down at her and did not blink. Somewhere behind them the water lapped once at the stones, a soft, hollow *tok*, and went quiet.

{A} breathed in through her nose. Wet earth, moss, the faint sharp smell of the other fighter carried down the slope on the breeze. Her heart had picked up without her asking it to. She let it.

*Then I'll come up. And you'll wish I hadn't.*

She set her weight forward onto the first of the dry ground, and felt it hold, and began to climb.

== strike act
{A} had been watching the same thing for a while now, and it had finally happened again.

Every time {B} shifted her weight to circle left, she let her guard drift for the length of a breath. Not much. Just enough that the near side of her body, the flank below the shoulder, came open and stayed open while her feet found the ground. {A} had seen it once and doubted it. She had seen it twice and stopped doubting.

*Now. While she's looking at her footing.*

She did not wind up. Winding up would have shown. She simply let go of the stillness she had been holding and went, low and straight across the packed earth, faster than she had moved all fight. The ground went by under her in a blur of dust and broken stems. She felt her own breath leave her in a hard push and not come back yet.

{B}'s head came round. {A} saw her eyes find the threat and widen, saw the body begin to turn to meet it, and knew the turn would be late. It was already late. The flank was still there, still open, a hand's width of it, and {A} put the whole of her rush into that hand's width.

{B} took it. The stone under her rolled. It was a small thing, a shift no wider than a finger, but {A} saw her body jerk to correct it, saw her attention drop for an instant to the ground.

== strike act
{B} was tired, and she was trying not to show it. {A} could see it anyway.

It caught her leg, the one she had been favouring, square across the place that had been hurt before, and {B} screamed.

She had not known she was going to. The sound simply tore out of her, high and raw, and the leg went out from under her as if it had been taken away, and she went down hard on her side on the stones at the water's edge. Cold spray came up round her. She barely felt it.

What she felt was the leg. It was all she felt. The hurt in it had been a steady, grinding ache before, something she could carry; now it had become a blaze, enormous and deep, a throbbing so fierce she could feel it in her jaw and behind her eyes. *Can't. Can't stand on that. Can't.*

{B} could not get up. She knew that with the whole of her body. But she could still move the rest of her, and she did: she dragged herself back a little way along the stones, away, the hurt leg trailing, every scrape of it against the ground sending a fresh bolt up into her hip.

She stopped. She turned her face up.

*Look at her. Don't look at the leg. Look at her.*

She looked. Her jaw was shaking, her breath still coming in those hitching heaves, and in her eyes, under the tears, there was still something hard and bright that had not gone out.

== strike take
It landed in the soft place below her ribs, and {B} grunted, a short heavy sound forced up out of her through a shut mouth.

== strike take

== knockdown take

== knockdown take
The blow took her legs out from under her, and {B} hit the ground on her back so hard the sky jolted.

== knockdown take

== chain
{A} did not stop after the first one. She had decided she would not, before she ever threw it.

The opening blow went high, at the head, quick and light. It was not meant to land hard; it was meant to make {B} look up. {B} looked up. Her guard came up with her eyes, the way it always did, and {A} was already moving through the blow and out the other side of it.

== chain
It began with a feint, and {A} meant every part of it to look real.

== grab
{B} had come in too close, and {A} decided to keep her there.

It was not a choice made in thought. It was made in the body. The other fighter had pressed forward after her last blow, eager, overextended, her weight a fraction too far forward, and {A} felt the opening the way one feels a step that is not there in the dark. == getup
{A} lay on her side and listened to her own breathing until it sounded like hers again.

{A} came up out of the shallows all at once, before she could think about how much it would hurt, because thinking about it would have kept her down.

== escape

{B} had been holding her for a long time, and she was tired. {A} could feel it. == escape aftermath
She was out, and {A}'s body did not seem to know it yet.

She breathed. It helped a little.

The rest of her was coming back in pieces. The shoulder that had been held down was stiff and cold; she rolled it and felt the blood come back into it in a rush of pins and needles that made her hiss. Her back was sore from the hard ground. Her leg, the one that had been trapped, trembled when she put weight on it, and she shifted the weight off again and let it rest.

She did not go back in. Not yet. She backed away instead, a step at a time, and kept her eyes on {B} the whole while.

{B} was on her feet now, and she was watching {A} too. Her breathing was hard. The escape had cost her something, a blow she had not seen coming, and {A} could see her holding the place where it had landed.

*Good. That's yours to carry now.*

{A} stopped when the distance felt right. She stood with her weight even and her body loose, letting the stiffness work its way out. The pins and needles faded from her shoulder. The tremor in her leg steadied.

She would not let {B} pin her like that again. She knew the feel of it now: the weight, the slow press, the way it came down when she tried to rise. She would see it coming.

The ache in her ribs had settled to something she could fight on.

== faint
Sound was the last of the world to leave {B}.

The ground under her had stopped being a thing she could feel some time ago, and the weight on her had thinned to a vague pressure, like a blanket. The sky had gone soft at the edges and then soft all through. What she could still hear was the wind in the grass, and {A} breathing hard above her, and under it all her own breath, slow and small.

*Not finished. I'm not... I was going to...*

What she was going to do slipped away before she could find it.

She tried to lift her eyelids. They did not answer, and it did not seem to matter very much. Everything she had been fighting so hard against, the hurt, the weight, the shortness of breath, had gone quiet, as if a door had closed between it and her.

Her body went loose. All of it at once: the tension drained out of her shoulders, out of her hips, out of everything she had kept clenched all fight. Her head rolled a little to one side and stayed there.

Her chest went on rising and falling. Small, slow, even.

{A} felt it go out of her the way one feels a rope go slack. The stiffness under her, the faint push back against her weight, gone, both at once.

She did not move.

She knew how a body could lie still and wait; she had done it herself. So she kept her weight where it was, and watched the shut eyes, and listened. A breath. Another. She pressed no harder. She only waited for a twitch, a flinch, anything.

Nothing came.

She lifted her weight off slowly, a little at a time, and the chest beneath her rose easier. Then she stepped back, and stood, and looked down.

{B} lay on her back on the trampled ground, eyes closed, breathing. {A} watched her for a long moment, her own sides heaving, and then looked away at the sky.

== faint
{B} was still on her feet when it began. She would not have said so afterward. She would not remember.

She had been standing, swaying, her weight off the hurt side, her eyes on {A}. She had been thinking about the next step. She had been sure there would be a next step. Then the edges of the world began to darken, softly, like evening coming on, and the sound of {A}'s breathing grew very loud and then very far away.

*Wait. Not yet. Not...*

Her body did not wait. Her weight sagged. Her whole frame simply stopped holding itself up, and she went down slowly, almost gently, onto her side on the warm ground.

She lay there. Her eyes were half open. They were not looking at anything.

{A} stood where she was. She did not come closer. She had seen fighters fall before and get up again, and she watched now with her whole body ready, waiting for the trick.

{B}'s chest rose. Fell. Rose. Slow and shallow and steady.

Her eyes drifted the rest of the way shut.

A breeze came through the grass and stirred it against {B}'s still side, and she did not move. A leaf came down from the tree and landed near her face, and she did not move. The light lay across her, warm and unchanging.

*Done. She's done.*

{A} let her breath out. It shook a little on the way. She took a step closer, then another, and stood over the still shape and looked down at it, and saw nothing in it now but sleep.

The tension went out of her own body all at once, and she felt how tired she was, and how much it had cost. Her legs trembled. She let them.

She stayed there, standing, and kept watch.

== aftermath
{A} did not move for a long time after it was over.

She stood where the fight had left her, her weight uneven, her breath still coming hard. The clearing had gone very quiet. The wind moved in the tree and the grass and that was all. Somewhere a bird began to sing again, cautiously, as if testing whether it was safe.

The fight let go of her in pieces. Her breath slowed first, though every one still snagged at the same sore place in her side. Then her heart, coming down from its pounding to something heavy and slow. Then the trembling in her legs, which had been there so long she had stopped noticing it, and which she noticed now only as it eased.

What was left was the hurt. With nothing else to think about, she could feel all of it.

Her side, where she had been struck, was a deep, sullen ache that pulsed with her heartbeat. Her shoulder was stiff and hot. There was a long scrape along one flank that she had not noticed getting and that stung now in the breeze. Her whole body was sore, a broad tiredness that had sunk into every muscle and settled there.

*Won. You won. So why does it feel like this?*

She did not know. She did not try to know. She just stood there and let herself feel it.

After a while she took a step. It hurt. She took another, slowly, favouring the side, and walked a little way out of the trampled patch where the fight had been, onto clean grass, and stood there.

The grass was cool. The sun was warm. Her breath was nearly even.

*Rest soon. Not yet.*

She turned her head and looked back across the clearing at the still shape lying in the flattened grass, and stood there watching it breathe.

== aftermath
It was over, and {A} found she did not quite believe it.

She stood at the water's edge where the last of it had happened, and the water lapped at the stones round her feet, cold and indifferent. She did not step out of it. She did not do anything at all for a while. Her body had been ready for the next blow for so long that it did not know how to stop being ready.

*She's down. It's done. Let go.*

She let go a little at a time. Her shoulders came down from where they had been, up and tense, guarding. Her weight settled more evenly. Her breath, which had been quick and shallow, began to lengthen.

Then the hurt came in, as it always did, once there was room for it.

Her face throbbed, a slow swollen ache along one side that she could feel every time she moved her mouth. Her ribs were sore on the left, sore enough that she took her breaths carefully, not too deep. The hurt leg had stiffened while she stood still and now it would barely bend. And underneath it all a deep, heavy weariness, as if she had been carrying stones up a hill since morning.

She waded out of the water at last, slow and limping, and stood on the dry stones above it.

The wind was cool. It dried her slowly. She closed her eyes for a moment and felt it move over her and breathed.

*That was close. Closer than it should have been.*

She thought of the moment near the start when {B} had nearly put her down, and of the blow she had not seen coming, and her face tightened at the memory.

Then she opened her eyes and turned, slowly, to look at the one who had done all that.

== aftermath take
{B} lay where she had fallen, on her side in the trampled grass, and the clearing had gone still round her.

She was breathing. That was the first thing to look for, and it was there: her side rising, slow and even, and falling, and a pause, and rising again. Nothing like the quick, ragged gulping of before. It was the breathing of someone asleep at the bottom of a long, hard day.

Her body had gone loose. All the guarding she had done, all the bracing, had let go together, and she lay heavy and soft against the ground with nothing held back. Her head was turned a little to one side. Her eyes were closed. A stalk of grass had bent across her face and she had not moved to push it away.

The marks of the fight were all there to read. A swelling along her side where the hardest blows had landed, dark and puffed. A scrape on one shoulder, a thin line of dried blood. The leg she had favoured lay a little apart from the rest of her, as if it had been set down carefully and left.

A breeze came across the clearing and stirred the grass round her. She did not stir with it.

A small bird landed on the ground a little way off, looked at her with its head on one side, and hopped closer. Then it thought better of it and flew away.

The light was moving. The shadow of the tree had crept across the grass since the fight began, and its edge lay near her now, a cool line, not quite touching.

{A} stood a little way off and watched it creep. Her own body ached. She did not sit. She stood, stiffly, favouring her own hurts, and watched {B} breathe, and kept watch over her until she woke.

== aftermath take
The water had gone still round {B} where she lay.

She was on her back at the very edge of it, half on the stones and half in the shallows, and the little waves came in and touched her side and went out again without disturbing her. It had pulled at her at first, gently, as if trying to take her somewhere. Now it had given up and only lapped.

She was breathing. Slow, shallow, steady. Her chest rose and fell, rose and fell, and each breath was the same as the one before.

Her face was turned up to the sky. It was quiet now, all the tightness gone out of it, the jaw loose, the brow smooth. One side of it was swollen, puffed and tender-looking, and a thin trickle of blood had dried at the corner of her mouth. Her eyes were closed.

The fight was written on her. The bruises along her ribs, darkening. A long scrape down one side, crusted with grit. The limb she had guarded so carefully, all fight, now lying open and forgotten in the water, nobody asking anything of it.

A small fish came in close to her side, nosed at nothing, and darted away again.

The sun was going down behind the trees. The light lay low and gold across the water and across her, and her shadow stretched out long behind her on the stones.

{A} stood on the bank above her. She was hurting, all over, a deep tiredness under every ache. She did not come down to the water. She did not turn away either.

*Still breathing. Good.*

She had not expected to feel that. She did, though. She let herself feel it, and stood there in the gold light, and watched the slow rise and fall of {B}'s chest, and waited for her to stir.

== clash
{A} saw it coming straight at her and did not move out of its way.

She went to meet it.

Both of them had chosen the same moment. Both of them had put everything into it, and now they came together in the middle of the open ground with a sound like stones slammed together, a deep, flat *crack* that went out across the clearing and came back off the trees.

The shock of it went through {A} from the front to the back. Every part of her jarred at once. She felt the ground slide under her as she was driven back a step, then caught herself, braced, and leaned in.

*Hold. Don't give her an inch.*

For a long moment neither of them gave. They were locked there, pushing, straining, each one's force pressed flat against the other's, and the effort of it burned through {A}'s whole body, a fierce, shaking heat. She could hear {B} breathing, harsh and loud, close. She could feel her own breath grinding out of her.

The ground scored under their feet. Dust rose round them in a slow cloud.

{A} found something. A little more. Somewhere deep, past where the effort burned, there was a last reserve, and she reached down and found it and threw it forward.

The balance tipped.

She felt it go. Felt {B}'s force give, just slightly, then a little more, then all at once, and her own push went through where the other's had been and carried on. {B} was knocked back. Not far. A stumble, a step, a hard landing on her feet.

{A} stood where the clash had been, swaying, breathing hard. Her body ached from the impact everywhere at once, a deep, all-over soreness.

*Stronger. I was stronger. Just.*

She did not let {B} see how close it had been. She set her weight, and lifted her head.

== clash
They went at each other at the same instant, and {A} knew it the moment she moved.

There was no time to change it. She had already committed, all her weight going forward, and across from her {B} was coming too, and the space between them closed so fast there was nothing to do but finish it.

They met.

The impact was enormous. {A} felt it in her skull, a deep ringing knock that went through her from front to back and left her ears humming. The air was full of dust. For a moment she could not see, could not hear, could only feel the force of {B} pressed flat against her own, both of them straining, neither of them moving.

*Push. Push through.*

She pushed. Her whole body was shaking with it. The ground was sliding under her, slowly, gravel crunching, and she dug in and stopped it sliding and pushed harder.

And something gave. Not in her. In {B}.

She felt it as a change in the pressure, a sudden lessening, the way a door gives when the person holding it shut lets go. {A}'s push went through all at once and she lurched forward with it, off balance for an instant, and {B} was thrown back and sideways and went down hard on one side.

{A} caught herself. Barely. She stood with her legs wide and her breath roaring in her ears and her whole body ringing like a struck bell.

*Both of us felt that. But she felt it more.*

{A} did not feel like a winner. Her body was one great ache from the impact, every muscle jarred. She drew herself up anyway, carefully, and stood straight, and let {B} see her standing.

== clash take
{B} felt it coming back up through her before she understood what had happened.

She had put everything into it. It was weaker than it would have been. It was enough. It took her square and knocked her back off her feet, and she landed hard on her side with the breath going out of her in a startled *hah*.

The worst of it was not the hurt. It was the knowing. She had thrown everything, and it had not been enough.

{A} was standing where the clash had been, not even knocked back. Or barely. Breathing hard, watching her.

== clash take
The two of them met in the middle, and {B} lost.

She knew it almost at once. The impact went through her and she felt her force stop, stall, push against something that pushed back harder. For a heartbeat they were matched. Then they were not. The rest of {A}'s strength came through. It hit her in the chest and shoulders and drove her back, sliding, her feet scoring the ground, until she lost her footing entirely and went down.

== dive
{A} went up until the ground was a flat green field far below her and {B} was a small dark shape on it, turning, looking for her.

She hung there at the top of her climb, light, the wind cold and steady against her face. It was quiet up here. The sounds of the clearing had fallen away, and all she could hear was the air moving past her and her own heart, slow and certain.

{B} did not look up. She was turning in a slow circle, searching the edges of the clearing, the tree, the rocks.

{A} dropped.

She let herself fall straight down, everything pulled in tight, and the air rose round her to a roar and then a shriek. The ground came up fast. The small dark shape grew and grew, sprouted a head and a body, began to turn its face upward at the sound.

== devastating
{A} had been waiting for it the whole fight, and when it came it came all at once.

{B} slipped. It was nothing, a loose stone rolling under her, a lurch to one side to catch herself. But the lurch turned her, and the turn opened the side she had been nursing since the early blows, the swollen, beaten place, and it was there in front of {A}, bare, for a single breath.

{A} was already moving. She did not choose it; her body had chosen it long ago and only now been given leave. Every scrap of strength she had left went down into the ground and came back up through her and out into the blow, and she aimed it at the exact centre of the old hurt.

It landed.

She knew at once that it was different. The earlier blows had all met something, a brace, a flinch, a tightening that soaked up some of the force and sent it back. This one met none of that. There was nothing left in that place to meet it with. She felt the blow sink, and keep sinking, and felt {B}'s whole body buckle round it as if the string holding her up had been cut.

*That's the one. That went all the way through.*

A strange calm came over her. She held still with the blow still pressed home, for no longer than a heartbeat, and felt {B} shudder against it.

{B} did not follow her. {B} did not do anything. She went down where she stood, folding slowly, and the noise she made as she went was small and thin and wavering, nothing like the cries from before.

{A} stood over her with her chest heaving and her own body ringing from the force of it. The clearing had gone silent. Even the wind seemed to have stopped to listen.

*She won't forget that. Neither will I.*

== devastating take
The stone rolled under {B}'s foot, and she had time to think *no* before it reached her.

It struck the old hurt. Not near it. Into it, into the swollen centre where every hard blow had gone before, and it arrived there with more behind it than any of them.

For a moment there was nothing. A strange, high emptiness, as if the world had drawn a breath and held it. Then the hurt came up out of that place and went through all of her at once, and it was so large that she could not find the edges of it.

She heard a sound. It took her a long moment to understand that it was coming out of her. It was not a cry. It was too thin for that, high and wavering and helpless, and it would not stop; it went on and on until the breath behind it ran out and left her mouth open and silent.

She was on the ground. Her body had folded round the hurt and locked there, and a deep, heavy shaking had started in her middle and was rolling out through every part of her in slow waves.

*Wrong. This is wrong. Something's wrong.*

She tried to breathe and the breath caught on the hurt and turned into a whimper. She tried again, smaller. That was a little better. Tiny sips, high in her chest, each one ending in a soft broken sound she could not keep in.

Everything else had gone far off. The ground under her cheek was rough and warm and very far away. The light was strange and grey. There was a ringing in her ears, steady, like an insect too close.

{B} could not lift her head to see. She could not do anything but lie curled round the place and shake, and breathe her tiny breaths, and wait for it to become something she could bear.

== breaking point
It found {B} where she had already been hurt too many times, and something in her gave.

She heard herself cry out before she felt it. High, raw, cracking in the middle, a sound that came out of her without asking permission. Then the feeling arrived, and it was not like the others. The others had been blows, each one sharp and then fading to an ache. This did not fade. It stayed. It grew. It filled her from the inside like water filling a hollow, and it pressed outward against her ribs and down into her hips and up into her throat, and it did not stop.

She was on the ground. She did not remember getting there.

Her body had curled round the hurt without her. Her limbs were drawn in, locked, shaking. A deep tremor ran through her in long waves, and her breath came in ragged little sobs, each one stopping short against the hurt and coming back out of her as a whimper.

*Not again. Not there. Please, not there.*

She heard the thought and hated it. She hated the whimper too. She clamped her mouth shut on the next one and it came out anyway, thin and broken, through her nose.

Her eyes were shut so tight that tears ran out of the corners. Her face was wet with them. She did not care.

Everything in her wanted to stay down. Stay curled. Stay small, so that nothing else could find her. The want was enormous and it was very tempting, and she lay there in it for a long moment, shaking, and almost gave in.
